115 年 8 月海關進出口貿易初步統計 
一、進出口貿易變動 
（一）出口 
8 月出口 824.0 億美元，創歷年單月新高，較上月增 9.4%，較上年
同月增 41.0%(按新臺幣計算增 52.6%)；累計 1 至 8 月出口 5,743.4
億美元，較上年同期增 44.2%(按新臺幣計算增 46.5%)。 
（二）進口 
8 月進口 601.0 億美元，為歷年單月第 3 高，較上月增 3.4%，較上
年同月增 44.3%(按新臺幣計算增 56.2%)；累 計1 至 8 月進口 4,374.3
億美元，較上年同期增 40.4%(按新臺幣計算增 42.4%)。 
（三）出入超 
8 月出超 223.0 億美元，為單月新高，較上年同月增 55.2 億美元；
累計 1 至 8 月出超 1,369.1 億美元，較上年同期增 500.5 億美元。

### Page 2

二、出口主要貨品 
（一）與上年同月比較 
受惠於人工智慧(AI)、 高效能運算及雲端服務等應用持續擴增，相關
產品出貨維持強勁， 加上價格攀升效 應，8月出口較上年同月增 239.7
億美元(+41.0%)，主要變動如下： 
1. 電子零組件出口 322.2 億美元(歷年單月新高)， 較上年同月增118.3
億美元(+58.0%)，其中積體電路增 115.5 億美元(+60.0%)、印刷電
路增 1.4 億美元(+29.9%)、 電容器及電阻器增0.7 億美元(+24.9%)。 
2. 資通與視聽產品出口 331.0 億美元，較上年同月增 97.6 億美元
(+41.8%)，其中電腦及其附屬單元增68.7 億美元(+40.4%)、儲存媒
體增 19.1 億美元(+2.4 倍)、 交換器及路由器增9.7 億美元(+1.1 倍)。 
3. 礦產品出口 20.0 億美元，較上年同月增 8.3 億美元(+70.2%)，其
中石油煉製品增 7.8 億美元(+74.3%)。 
4. 基本金屬及其製品出口 29.6 億美元，較上年同月增 5.4 億美元
(+22.4%)，其中銅及其製品增 6.1 億美元(+1.5 倍)，鋼鐵及其製品
減 1.1 億美元(-8.7%)。 
5. 機械出口 26.8 億美元，較上年同月增 3.6 億美元(+15.2%)，其中
生產半導體等機械增 1.9 億美元(+42.1%)、未列名具特殊功能機械
增 0.4 億美元(+39.0%)。

累計 1 至 8 月出口較上年同期增 1,759.8 億美元(+44.2%)，以資通與
視聽產品增 1,023.7 億美元(+68.5%)、電子零組件增 629.5 億美元
(+45.8%)增加較多。

生產等出現結構性轉變，爰另予併計。

三、進口主要貨品 
（一）與上年同月比較 
由於 AI 產業鏈國際分工與出口衍生需求之帶動，積體電路及資通產
品進口維持強勁，加上輸入物價明顯上漲，8 月總進口較上年同月增
184.5 億美元(+44.3%)，主要變動如下： 
1. 農工原料進口408.4 億美元， 較上年同月增129.4億美元(+46.4%)，
其中電子零組件增 82.5 億美元(+62.6%)、礦產品增 16.9 億美元
(+38.4%)、電腦之零附件增 14.6 億美元(+84.4%)、基本金屬增 4.5
億美元(+21.8%)、塑化原料增 4.0 億美元(+16.2%)。 
2. 資本設備進口 130.6 億美元， 較上年同月增 38.4 億美元(+41.6%)，
其中資通設備增 27.2 億美元(+1.2 倍)、半導體設備增 2.8 億美元
(+7.3%)、交通運輸設備增 2.8 億美元(+1.1 倍)。 
3. 消費品進口 55.7 億美元，較上年同月增 16.0 億美元(+40.5%)，其
中固態非揮發性儲存裝置增12.1 億美元(+3.7 倍)、藥粧用品增 1.2
億美元(+17.4%)、手機增 1.1 億美元(+78.1%)。

四、主要出口國家/地區 
（一）與上年同月比較 
8 月對陸港、日本出口創新高，對美國、東協為單月第 3 高 
1. 對美國出口 236.7 億美元，較上年同月增 40.6 億美元(+20.7%)，
其中資通與視聽產品增 23.0 億美元(+14.6%)、電子零組件增 11.6
億美元(+1.5 倍)、電機產品增 1.7 億美元(+30.1%)。 
2. 對中國大陸與香港出口 211.5 億美元，較上年同月增 59.7 億美元
(+39.3%)，其中電子零組件增 47.4 億美元(+45.6%)、資通與視聽
產品增 6.7 億美元(+42.9%)、 基本金屬及其製品增4.6 億美元(+1.2
倍)。 
3. 對東協出口 151.3 億美元，較上年同月增 44.9 億美元(+42.2%)，
其中電子零組件增 25.3 億美元(+50.3%)、資通與視聽產品增 12.6
億美元(+41.1%)、礦產品增 4.6 億美元(+1.5 倍)。 
4. 對歐洲出口 44.5 億美元，較上年同月增 11.5 億美元(+34.8%)，其
中資通與視聽產品增 6.6 億美元(+57.0%)、電子零組件增 4.0 億美
元(+1.1 倍)，基本金屬及其製品減 1.4 億美元(-31.0%)。 
5. 對日本出口 36.6 億美元，較上年同月增8.9 億美元(+32.0%)，其中
電子零組件增 8.9 億美元(+76.7%)、 化學品增0.5 億美元(+32.3%)，
資通與視聽產品減 0.6 億美元(-13.9%)。 
（二）與上年同期比較 
累計 1 至 8 月對美國出口 1,803.2 億美元，較上年同期增 631.6 億美
元(+53.9%)，對中國大陸與香港出口 1,394.7 億美元，增 307.5 億美
元(+28.3%)， 對東協出口1,150.7 億美元 ， 增372.3 億美元(+47.8%)，
對歐洲出口 376.6 億美元 ， 增130.7 億美元(+53.1%)， 對日本出口247.0
億美元，增 54.7 億美元(+28.4%)。

五、主要進口國家/地區 
（一）與上年同月比較 
8 月自中國大陸與香港進口 126.8 億美元 ， 較上年同月增47.6 億美元
(+60.2%)，自東協進口 106.9 億美元，增46.7 億美元(+77.6%)，自南
韓進口 91.7 億美元，增29.6 億美元(+47.7%)，自日本進口 57.0 億美
元，增 11.2 億美元(+24.4%)，自美國進口 53.9 億美元，增 14.3 億美
元(+35.9%)，自歐洲進口 49.5 億美元，增 6.2 億美元(+14.3%)。 
（二）與上年同期比較 
累計 1 至 8 月自主要國家/地區進口與上年同期比較 ， 自東協增75.4%、
自南韓增 63.2%、自中國大陸與香港增 40.5%、自美國增 24.4%、自
日本增 24.1%、自歐洲增 12.8%。

六、主要國家/地區貿易概況 
（一）今年 1-7 月主要國家/地區外貿景氣普遍上升，南韓(1-8 月)、香港、
新加坡出口動能尤為強勁，分別年增 52.8%、46.6%、38.3%；中國
大陸(1-8 月)、美國出口分別年增 19.3%及 14.6%，德國(1-6 月)、日
本各年增 10.9%、8.1%。 
（二）主要國家/地區進口同步上揚，累計 1-7 月以香港進口年增 48.1%最
為顯著， 新加坡、 中國大陸(1-8 月)分別年增 37.0%、27.0%，南韓(1-8
月)、德國(1-6 月)各增 18.7%、11.7%，日本、美國各增 6.5%、1.2%。

圖 5 2026 年主要國家/地區出進口年增率
七、綜合分析
（一）得益於人工智慧(AI)、高效能運算及 雲端服務等應用 需求強勁， 加
以外銷產品價格攀升，8 月出口 824.0 億美元，創歷年單月新高，年
增 41.0%，連續 34 個月正成長；進口 601.0 億美元，年增 44.3%，
主因 AI 產業鏈國際分工與出口衍生需求擴張 ，及農工原料行情上
漲；出、進口互抵後，出超 223.0 億美元，亦創單月新高 。累計 1
至 8 月出口年增 44.2%、進口增 40.4%。
（二）8 月出口主要貨品以電子零組件、資通與視聽產品貢獻最多，年增率
分別為 58.0%及 41.8%，前者受惠 AI 商機及記憶體外銷價量齊揚，規
模值創單月新高，兩類併計增 49.3%，其餘貨類平均增 16.3%，以礦
產品隨油品售價上漲，年增 70.2%較為突出，基本金屬及其製品、電
機產品各增 22.4%、22.3%。累計 1 至 8 月資通與視聽產品出口值已
超越歷年水準，與電子零組件合計占總出口比重達 78.7%。

（三）8 月對主要市場出口同呈雙位數成長，其中對中國大陸與香港、日本
受電子零組件外銷強勁推升，出口值同創單月新高，各年增 39.3%、
32.0%，對東協、歐洲、美國分別增 42.2%、34.8%、20.7%；五大市
場之外，對墨西哥及澳大利亞出口皆增逾 2 倍。累計 1 至 8 月對美
國、歐洲出口各年增 53.9%、53.1%，對東協年增 47.8%，對日本、
陸港各增 28.4%、28.3%，對五大市場出口規模均為歷年同期最佳。
（四）展望未來，地緣政治衝突 及美國關稅政策變化等不確定 因素，持續
干擾全球經濟成長力道，惟隨人工智慧應用 不斷深化，算力需求攀
升，國際雲端服務業者加速建置 AI 基礎設施，加以國內半導體及伺
服器等供應鏈具競爭優勢，均有助支撐我國外銷動能，下半年出口
可望維持成長態勢。

### Page 1

Taiwan Semiconductor Manufacturing Co.

TSMC-Musk: customer relationship intact; 
tech/control sharing with Terafab unlikely

Reiterate Rating: BUY | PO: 3,100 TWD | Price: 2,500 TWD

Customer neutrality central to TSMC’s foundry model 
Elon Musk on Saturday October 3rd responded “Just discussions, but something may come 
of it” to an article discussing the potential manufacturing partnership between TSMC 
and Terafab. From chipset design and foundry service perspective, we expect a deeper 
collaboration as Tesla and SpaceX grow at TSMC (2-3% of its sales) directly with their 
auto/robotics and cloud AI ASICs and indirectly through merchant HPC chipset buys (e.g. 
GPUs, networking switches) based on our estimate. However, the likelihood of TSMC 
licensing technology or operating a fab with Terafab holding a meaningful share should 
be low. Specifically, we would note TSMC’s JV fab record is limited including WaferTech, 
SSMC, VIS, JASM, ESMC and AVSM mostly on mature nodes. The company did not co-
own a fab with Apple who contributed ~21% of its revenue from ’15-25 mostly on the 
advanced processes as it keeps full business control and preserves customer neutrality. 
Terafab capex insufficient for 1 terawatt capacity target 
Based on Terafab’s applications under Texas’s JETI program, it outlined ~US$119bn of 
planned investment across 4 phases from ’26-36, with ~60% of it on tool procurement. 
The spending in ’26-28 would be highly skewed toward facility construction before it 
starts to pull in US$6-8bn equipment per annum from ‘29-36. Based on our estimate of 
US$600mn+ for every 1k WPM expansion on the advanced nodes in the US, Terafab 
could only grow its capacity installed base by ~10k WPM per annum at maximum even if 
it can successfully deliver the technology roadmap through its own R&D effort and Intel 
partnership. This would prevent Terafab’s operation from the economies of scale as the 
node migration continues, keeping TSMC as the major foundry partner for Tesla/SpaceX. 
Stock at mid-cycle supported by robust AI demand pipeline 
We keep our Buy rating on TSMC with PO at NT$3,100 on 20x '27 P/E (vs. current 16x).

Company Sector 
Semiconductors

Company Description 
TSMC was founded in 1987 and is listed in Taiwan 
(2330TT) and the US (TSM/NYS).TSMC is the largest and 
global leader in integrated circuit (IC) manufacturing. As a 
build-to-order foundry, it provides a wide range of value-
add activities: IC manufacturing, mask-making, IC design 
services, turnkey solutions, and process development. We 
attribute its success to its proven, winning business model, 
unparalleled scale advantage, optimized execution, and 
technology scope and depth. 5 shares = 1 ADR.

Investment Rationale 
The ongoing semi content growth in mobile, rise of artificial 
intelligence (AI), and proliferation of Internet of Things (IoT) 
should result in sustainable upside in aggregate computing 
power globally. TSMC, as the leading contract manufacturer 
of semiconductor chips, is in a good position to capitalize. 
We are structurally positive on TSMC and expect it to 
sustain over 15% growth with rising FCF from 2026 
onwards.

### Page 4

Price objective basis & risk 
Taiwan Semiconductor Manufacturing Co. (TSMWF / TSM) 
Our PO for TSMC is NT$3,100 per share (US$590 per ADR), based on 20x 2027E P/E. 
TSMC's valuation has traded in the range of 10-26x PE from 2017. 20x is in the mid to 
high end of the historical range, above the average P/E of 18x. We think a PE above the 
average is justified given TSMC's enhanced industry position and mid-40% 5-year CAGR 
in AI should underpin a rerating

Downside risks to our price objective are: (1) greater-than-expected slowdown in global 
smartphone/consumer electronics demand, (2) Intel's potential insourcing strategy and 
ambitions in foundry service, (3) execution risks on advanced nodes, and (4) higher than 
expected tariffs.

Initiation of Coverage
Hermes Testing Solutions
Shifting-left, scaling-up: dual catalysts in chip
probing and CPO
Jack-of-all-trades for bespoke testing solutions in the semiconductor industry
We initiate coverage of Hermes Testing (HTSI) with a Buy rating and NT$8,880 PT. HTSI is
uniquely positioned to benefit from two key semiconductor testing trends: shift-left
testing and the emerging CPO testing market. Leveraging its long-standing relationship

with TSMC, HTSI has established capabilities across electrical test engineering,
customised test equipment, thermal control modules, probe cards, and CPO testing
solutions. We forecast revenue growth of 127%/152%/78% in 2026E/27E/28E, driven
by increasing adoption of thermal control modules for wafer-level test and initial
deployment of CPO insertion 2 and insertion 3 test equipment through ficonTEC.
Additional 2028E growth drivers include reflow tools, laser clean solutions, and MEMS

probe cards. The stock trades at 27/15x 2027/28E PE, versus ~35x NTM PE for handler-
equipment peers, offering re-rating potential as sales scale, new products are qualified,
and its CPO presence expands. See our companion testing deep dive sector note.
Shift-left beneficiary with exceptional thermal control modules
Thermal control modules should be HTSI's largest growth driver over the next several
years. We expect increasing chip power consumption, rising package complexity, and

tighter probe pitches to drive adoption of advanced thermal control solutions used
alongside leading-edge probers, in additional to more wafer level insertions and longer
test time. We estimate HTSI will ship approximately 3,000 thermal-control modules in
2027E, up from around 1,200 units in 2026E, sales contribution rise from ~30% in
Q226 to >50% of sales by 2027E. The launch of its second-generation platform in
2027E, which carries 25-50% higher ASPs, should further improve profitability. We

forecast GM expansion from 55.5% in 2026E to 58.3%/60.0% in 2027E/28E.
Unique position to capture CPO upside
Through its partnership with German optical-automation leader ficonTEC, HTSI is
positioned to participate in the initial ramp of CPO testing equipment. HTSI supports
local manufacturing, installation, automation and after-sales service, making it a key
enabler of volume production. We estimate HTSI captures approximately 30% of
equipment value across the 40 insertion 2 and 70 insertion 3 systems we forecast for

delivery in 2027E, with further potential upside in 2028E as adoption expands.
Valuation: initiate coverage with a Buy rating and price target NT$8,880
We forecast EPS of NT$29.1 in 2026E (including a one-off employee share purchase
programme impact), rising to NT$153.3/286.5 in 2027E/28E. Our NT$8,880 price target
is based on 31x average 2028E PE, versus handler-equipment peers forward PE at
roughly 35x, reflecting HTSI's unique exposure to both shift-left testing, CPO, and our

estimate of 32% LT earnings CAGR. We initiate with a Buy rating.

Q: Will HTSI be able to keep its moat within chip probing thermal control modules?
Yes. We expect HTSI's competitive position to strengthen as chip power consumption, package
complexity, and probe density continue to increase. The company is developing next-generation

thermal control solutions, including liquid cooling technologies, while also expanding into specialized
chuck solutions designed to address wafer warpage. In addition, HTSI's close collaboration with
TSMC, TEL, Advantest, and other OSATs should reinforce its position within advanced wafer-level
testing.
Q: Will insertion 2 of CPO testing process be skipped?
No. We believe concerns surrounding insertion 2 adoption are overdone. While some industry

participants favor bypassing insertion 2 due to throughput and ROI considerations, doing so increases
the risk of defective optical engines being identified only after the components are delivered to
OSATs, creating yield accountability challenges and higher packaging losses. Based on our industry
studies, we expect insertion 2 testing to remain an important part of the CPO workflow over at least
the next two years as the industry focuses on improving PIC yields and overall testing efficiency.

We initiate coverage of Hermes Testing (HTSI) with a Buy rating and NT$8,880 price target. HTSI
offers unique exposure to two of the semiconductor testing industry's most attractive growth drivers:
1) shift-left testing and 2) CPO testing. The industry's shift-left testing strategy will have more test
insertions move to the wafer level before packaging, in addition to longer CP time. Leveraging its
longstanding relationship with TSMC, HTSI has established capabilities across thermal control

modules, electrical-test engineering services, customized equipment, and emerging CPO testing
solutions. We believe there could be re-rating potential as sales scale, new products are qualified, and
its CPO presence expands.
TSMC and OSATs have been expanding their CoWoS capacities aggressively, from 40kwpm in 2024
to around 90 kwpm in 2025, and is tracking to expand CoWoS capacity toward 160kwpm in 2026E.
TSMC also ramped up expanding N3/N2 capacity, with multiple product launches from customers'

pipelines—Nvidia's Rubin, AMD's MI400 series/Venice CPU and other cloud ASICs—translating into
foreseeable, strong testing demand.
Despite a 264% YTD share-price increase, HTSI currently trades at just 15x of our 2028E EPS versus
handler-equipment peers forward PE at roughly 35x. We believe market expectations for 2027 CPO
testing equipment shipments remain conservative, particularly for insertion 2 equipment. Based on
our industry analysis, we estimate HTSI will deliver 40 insertion 2 and 70 insertion 3 systems in 2027E,

this should be higher than the market's expectation of no insertion 2 and limited insertion 3 shipment
due to engineering bottleneck. We also see further upside in 2028E as CPO adoption expands. Our
reverse DCF analysis indicates that the current market price implies only a 17% long-term sales CAGR
vs our estimate of 27% and long-term GM level of 33% vs our estimate of 61%. We see scope for
re-rating.

Hermes Testing Solutions (HTSI) was founded in 2004 in Hsinchu, Taiwan. It focuses on providing

semiconductor testing and inspecting solutions, equipment installation, technical support, and
maintenance services. Its main business segments are customized semiconductor equipment,
engineering services, and probe cards and other materials.

Hermes Testing Solutions UBS Research
Testing TAM expanding with IC complexity: leaders likely to
outperform industry growth
Our central view is that testing content per AI accelerator is growing faster than
semiconductor unit volumes, creating a multi-year expansion in testing TAM. We believe
semiconductor testing is entering a period of structural growth as AI-driven chip
complexity increases testing intensity across wafer, package and system levels. The

industry's shift-left strategy, which moves more testing upstream in the semiconductor
manufacturing process, is resulting in longer and more frequent test insertions. As cloud
AI GPUs and ASICs become increasingly complex, additional testing is required to ensure
functionality, performance and yield, while reducing defective parts per million (DPPM).
More robust testing should improve deployment efficiency and lower the cost of
operating AI infrastructure.

Semiconductor testing generally comprises four stages: wafer/chip probing (CP), final
test (FT), system-level test (SLT), and burn-in. CP is conducted at the wafer-level before
packaging, while FT, SLT, and burn-in occur after packaging. The below Figure 3S e m i c o n d u c t o r  t e s t i n g  p r o c e s s :  f r o m  w a f e r  l e v e l  t o  p a c k a g e  l e v e l
illustrates the semiconductor manufacturing flow.
Figure 1: Semiconductor testing process: from wafer level to package level

We estimate semiconductor testing historically accounts for ~1% of total
semiconductor revenue. Within testing, consumables such as probe cards, sockets and
interface boards represent ~10-15% of industry revenue. According to Yole, the probe
card, socket and interface board markets should grow from US$3.8bn, US$2.3bn and
US$1.7bn in 2026 to US$5.5bn, US$3.1bn and US$2.0bn by 2031, respectively.

Nonetheless, leaders within the testing sector will standout among competition, with

We see HTSI's sales for the next few years to mainly be driven by 1) thermal control
module shipment and 2) CPO equipment shipment. In the past, thermal control

modules weren't necessary for chip probing, due to less TDP for the chips. However, as
new iterations of AI accelerators now got substantial power draw, this unlocks a brand
new TAM for HTSI to enter with their thermal control modules to pair with probers, in
order to prevent probe card damages.
 Robust thermal control module shipments.  We estimate HTSI to ship
3,000/4,100 thermal control modules in 2027E/28E, with continuous demand in
future years, bringing their 2026-31E semiconductor equipment sales CAGR to

48%.

Robust growth drivers across semiconductor testing
We believe HTSI is set to benefit from several semiconductor testing growth drivers and
capture the AI induced, expanding testing TAM. We see HTSI is uniquely positioned as
the main beneficiary of two of the strongest growth drivers within semiconductor
testing: testing shift-left and CPO.
Shift-left igniting secular growth for chip probing

### Page 5

Hermes Testing Solutions 8 October 2026 ab 5
The industry's adoption of shift-left testing should drive a multi-year structural growth
opportunity for chip probing (CP) and its supply chain. As advanced packaging costs rise
and AI accelerators become increasingly complex, semiconductor manufacturers are
placing greater emphasis on identifying known-good dies (KGD) before packaging.
Figure 4: CP is set to grow substantially with shift-left, each packaging step set to be MORE costly than the prior step

RDL / RWAT testing Wafer-level CP (WLCP)
CoWChiplet (if use SoIC) CoW
CP (Die A)
CP (Die B)
Assembly Test (AST)
oS
Source: UBS
The economics of advanced packaging reinforce this trend. Heterogeneous integration,
chiplet architectures and 2.5D/3D packaging significantly increase package value, while
defects identified late in the manufacturing process can lead to substantial scrap costs.
To mitigate this risk, foundries and OSATs such as TSMC and ASE employ distributed

testing flows incorporating KGD, known-good-stack (KGS) and assembly test screening,
to catch defects before the components enter a multi-week packaging cycle (>2 weeks
for CoW and 2 weeks for oS). The importance of these tests rises as AI clusters become
increasingly dependent on component reliability and low defective-parts-per-million
(DPPM) rates. Given the multiplicative yield degradation, a 300bps yield decline at just
one manufacturing stage (e.g. dropping from 99.5% to 96.5%) reduces overall

integrated four-stage yield from 98.0% to 95.0%; on a 500,000 unit ramp at US
$10,000 per unit, this 3% scrap delta translates into around US$150m in unrecoverable
losses given >6-month manufacturing lead times.
At the same time, rising chip power consumption is increasing the technical difficulty of
wafer-level testing. As chip thermal design power scales beyond 1,000W and power
density is expected by the industry to surge another 8x by 2030, traditional wafer-level

chip probe (WLCP) encounters severe physical bottlenecks. Finer probe pitches and
higher power densities increase the risk of overheating, probe needle burnout and yield
degradation, creating demand for more advanced probing and thermal-control
solutions.

Hermes Testing (HTSI) is a key beneficiary of this trend. Leveraging its long-standing
relationship with TSMC and expertise in semiconductor test solutions, HTSI supplies

advanced thermal control modules used alongside leading-edge probers (made by
Tokyo Electron), particularly for AI applications. These thermal control modules are
required for high TDP semiconductor wafer probe in order to maintain the temperature

### Page 6

Hermes Testing Solutions 8 October 2026 ab 6
of the chuck to avoid needle burns or probe card damages. We estimate HTSI will ship
approximately 3,000 thermal-management modules in 2027E, up from 1,200 in 2026E,
supporting sales growth of 127%/152% in 2026E/27E. HTSI is also the partner for
prober installation, prober maintenance and services. In addition, HTSI provides high-
end chuck processing services, an area that should benefit from increasing package sizes

and higher wafer warpage associated with advanced packaging.
Beyond thermal control modules, HTSI also supplies consumables and customised
solutions for wafer-level testing, including probe card cleaning sheets and laser-based
probe head cleaning systems. As probing intensity increases under the shift-left testing
model, demand for cleaning and maintenance solutions should rise alongside probe
card utilisation. Currently, cleaning sheets is around 5% of sales, but we believe it could

gain traction in 2027/28E and beyond. We see additional upside from future modular
laser cleaning systems that could be integrated directly with probers, further expanding
HTSI's content opportunity within advanced wafer-level testing.
CPO testing fuelling future growth for semiconductor testing
As interconnect bottlenecks increasingly shift from the memory wall to the copper wall,
we believe co-packaged optics (CPO) adoption is inevitable over the longer term. Testing

remains one of the key bottlenecks to CPO deployment, as optical validation differs
fundamentally from the electrical testing processes that have dominated semiconductor
manufacturing for decades. As a result, CPO is creating a new testing opportunity across
the semiconductor equipment and consumables ecosystem. We have written
extensively on CPO and opportunities that it brings to the semiconductor supply chain,
see our CPO deep dive , our thoughts on CPO timing , 2026 SiPhIA conference

takeaways, and SEMICON 2026 takeaways.
Various test equipment suppliers are scrambling to come up with ideal testing
equipment across the four insertions of CPO testing, with solutions to ensure both
optical and electrical signal integrity of the CPO solutions. The four insertions are:
 Insertion 1: Electronic IC (EIC)/Photonic IC (PIC) test.  Single-side wafer test
that has dedicated probe cards to test the fabricated EIC/PIC wafers.
 Insertion 2: Optical/Electrical test. Double sided wafer test, testing the hybrid

bonded (e.g. via TSMC's COUPE) EIC and PIC wafers by an opto-electrical test that
verifies the electrical signals on the top and optical signal on the bottom of the
hybrid bonded wafers.
 Insertion 3: Optical engine (OE) test.  The OE tests are done to ensure the
quality of the OEs and with FAU (Fiber Array Unit) attached, to ensure the signal
integrity of the OEs. The testing equipment needs to be able to handle 224G per
channel data rates, requiring high unit per hour (UPH) throughput, meticulous dirt

mitigation, and maintaining a near 100% contact rating on high-speed sockets.
 Insertion 4: ASIC final test.  This final test step is established post the OEs are
packaged together with the ASIC. The complete ASIC package (now with CPO) is
now tested for opto-electrical functionalities.
As it stands, insertion 2 and insertion 3 are the two areas where there are the most
debates in the industry. Insertion 3 suppliers such as Chroma has noted the trend of

“shift-to-middle” paradigm centered on insertion 3. In high-density scale-out switches
deploying up to 32 optical engines (e.g. Nvidia’s Spectrum-X architectures), any OE
defect results in package scrap at the CoWoS level, making 100% known good die
(KGD) validation at the singulated OE stage an absolute commercial requirement.

With its 25 years of precision test equipment expertise, ficonTEC is an early mover for
CPO test equipment and has key know-how for optical alignment, which is
quintessential for optical test. HTSI, through its partnership with ficonTEC, is set to be a
major beneficiary once CPO proliferates in 2027E-28E. As the partner of ficonTEC,
Hermes Testing is the key enabler that can help ficonTEC achieve HVM for its CPO

testing equipment. As of now, our view is that both insertion 2 and insertion 3 should be
required within the next two years, despite the difficulties surrounding double-sided
wafer probing (insertion 2), as the yield for OEs are still not high enough to avoid
defective dies. Finding out known-good OEs will be crucial before the FAU coupling
process during the latter stages of CPO packaging, to prevent impeding the final
package with multiple OEs attached to the switch ASIC (e.g. 32 OEs on Nvidia's

Spectrum-X). We estimate Hermes Testing will ship 40/70 units of insertion 2/3 testing
equipment in 2027E, with potential upside to the numbers as we view Hermes Testing
will likely assist with the ramp up for Gen 2 insertion 3 equipment in 2027E. Gen 2
insertion equipment will come with better throughput and ASP .

Beyond insertions 2 and 3, HTSI is also positioned to participate in insertion 1 testing
through customised prober solutions for photonic integrated circuit (PIC) testing. We
believe these solutions could eventually be packaged as licensable intellectual property,

creating an additional long-term growth opportunity as CPO adoption broadens. While
near-term testing growth is driven by AI accelerators, CPO represents the next major
expansion cycle for semiconductor testing. We believe HTSI is uniquely positioned to
participate across multiple test insertions, providing exposure to a potentially significant
source of incremental revenue beginning in 2027E.

HTSI's unique position for new TAM of thermal control
modules and CPO test equipment
Hermes Testing Solutions (HTSI) was founded in 2004 in Hsinchu, Taiwan, as a spin-off
from Hermes-Epitek. The company provides semiconductor testing and inspection

solutions, engineering services, customized equipment, and test consumables. HTSI
maintains a global service footprint across Taiwan (Hsinchu, Tainan), Mainland China
(Suzhou), Japan (Tokyo, Kumamoto), Singapore, Malaysia, and the United States
(Arizona), enabling close support for leading semiconductor customers.
Its main business segments are structured around three pillars:
 Semiconductor equipment and customized products : HTSI utilises a dual-

track strategy of independent R&D and also equipment agency sales. They offers

### Page 9

Hermes Testing Solutions 8 October 2026 ab 9
highly customized solutions designed to address specific client pain points and
bottlenecks. Key products include: thermal control modules that pair with a 1:1
ratio with wafer-level test probers, electron beam inspection equipment, white
light interference optical inspection systems, laser probe cleaning machines, and
the SYMPHONY fluxless reflow system.
 Testing equipment engineering services:  The company operates the largest

engineering service team in Taiwan, consisting of over 340 technical professionals.
The engineering service team provides extensive equipment life-cycle support,
including installation, relocations, modification, testing, routine maintenance, and
repair services. CPO related equipment sales/services will be recognized within this
segment, as CPO are not direct HTSI products, but rather sales recognition via its
partnership with ficonTEC.
 Probe card design, manufacturing, and cleaning materials:  HTSI delivers a

comprehensive product portfolio that supports the entire probe card life-cycle. For
probe cards, they supply membrane, cantilever, and vertical probe cards. As they
are small in scale, HTSI targets niche probe card applications such as RF, 5G/6G,
millimeter wave, LCD driver, low end logics, TIA (Transimpedance Amplifier), etc. In
addition to the three existing probe technologies, HTSI also collaborates with MJC
in Japan for MEMS probe development, targeting sales in 2028E for high-end, AI/

HPC related applications. Cleaning materials is another important business within
this segment, as HTSI source and process sand paper or other materials to offer
cleaning sheets to clean the probe cards, removing oxidations, unwanted particles
and substances that accumulated on the probe cards during the testing process.
The sales resiliency in the coming years mainly stems from its robust relationship with key
industry players, especially as an engineering and service provider with TSMC for testing

solutions. This sets up a solid foundation for its customized equipment business, as they
know the specific testing bottleneck to tackle. For instance, as Hermes-Epitek
manufacturers the probers for Tokyo Electron (TEL) and HTSI helps with installation,
services, and maintenance of the probers, they understand the thermal bottlenecks that
the wafer-level test industry has been facing and can effectively come up with module
solutions to solve this.

Margins set to expand with value-added products and services
The company has demonstrated strong execution in advanced wafer-level testing, with
H126 revenue of NT$2.2bn already exceeding 90% of FY25 revenue (NT$2.4bn).
Profitability has also improved significantly, with GM increasing from 42.1% in 2025 to
54.9% in H126.
With share of value-added products and services set to rise in 2027E in sales mix, HTSI is
set to see GM and operating leverage expansion. As a niche engineering service provider

that has robust ties to leading foundries and OSATs, we believe HTSI has substantial
pricing power and estimate HTSI' GM for thermal control modules to be around 65%,
with GM for CPO equipment of around 60% as HTSI provides an engineering-service-
like business for it. We believe these factors will help the company to reach a 60% long-
term GM range.
OPM is also set to expand with customized equipment sales rise in total sales mix. If we
exclude the one-time expense to be recognized during Q326 for employee stock

benefits (we estimate around NT$1bn), YoY opex growth will be around 99% for
2027E, with downside risk if the company conducts tight cost control measures. We
forecast the company's OPM will expand from 19.5% in 2026E, to 44.3%/47.0% in
2027/28E.

Despite a 264% YTD share-price increase, HTSI still trades at just 15x 2028E P/E. We
believe market expectations for 2027E CPO testing equipment shipments remain
conservative, particularly for insertion 2 equipment. Based on our industry analysis, we
estimate HTSI will deliver 40 insertion 2 and 70 insertion 3 systems in 2027E, this should
be higher than the market's expectation of no insertion 2 and limited insertion 3

shipment due to engineering bottleneck. We also see further upside in 2028E as CPO
adoption expands. Our reverse DCF analysis indicates that the current market price
implies only a 17% long-term sales CAGR vs our estimate of 27% and long-term GM
level of 33% vs our estimate of 61%. We see scope for re-rating.
We believe the market is not fully reflecting three key growth drivers:
1) Shift-left testing is still underappreciated.
As advanced packaging costs rise and AI accelerators become increasingly complex,

more thorough testing is done at the wafer-level. This should drive sustained demand
for HTSI's thermal control modules, probe card consumables, and other wafer-level
testing solutions.
2) CPO revenue potential remains underestimated.
Investor expectations for 2027 CPO equipment shipments, particularly insertion 2
testing systems, remain conservative. Based on our industry studies, we forecast HTSI
will deliver 40 insertion 2 and 70 insertion 3 systems in 2027E, with further potential

upside in 2028E as CPO adoption broadens.
3) New products provide additional optionality.
Beyond its core businesses, HTSI is expanding into higher-value solutions including mass-
reflow tools, MEMS probe cards and laser cleaning systems, creating incremental
growth opportunities that we believe are largely absent from current expectations.
We value HTSI using 31x average 2028E PE, underpinned by a 32% 2027-31E earnings
CAGR. We use 2028E PE as our view is that 2028 is the year for thermal control modules

and CPO adoption grow from the initial ramping up stage to mass adoption stage. We
believe using handlers as a peer group is justifiable for a unique company like HTSI, with
thermal control modules for chip probing sharing similar characteristics as active thermal
control used for final test, in addition to Hon Precision and Chroma having exposure to
CPO test equipment. For equipment vendors, the peer group's average NTM PE post
2022 is around 35x, and the group is currently trading at around 41x NTM PE (see

What's Priced In chart).
HTSI is trading at NT$4,190 as of 6
Oct 2026

Risk to the current share price is heavily skewed (2.2:1) to the upside.
UPSIDE (NT$10,000): Our upside scenario reflects stronger than expected thermal
module and CPO equipment shipment in 2027-28E, with additional upside from better
traction of probe cards and mass reflow tools, forming 157%/91% YoY sales growth in
2027/28E and even higher operating leverage with OPM of 46.3%/50.0% in 2027/28E.

This scenario implies a 34x 2028E PE, a upside driving by more equipment demand
stemming for robust shift-left test induced equipment bottleneck, alongside CPO
proliferation with scale-up CPO equipment pull-in in 2028E.
BASE (NT$8,880): Our price target of NT$8,880 is based on 31x 2028E PE. We expect an
earnings CAGR of 32% in 2027-31E. With shift-left trend for more thermal module
requirement during testing, alongside 40/70 CPO insertion 2/3 shipment. We see these

growth drivers driving YoY sales growth from a high base of 127% in 2026E to
152%/78% YoY in 2027/2028E, with OPM of 44.3%/47.0% in 2027/28E.
DOWNSIDE (NT$1,500): Our downside scenario reflects lower shipment of thermal
control modules due to less advanced prober demand with less insertion 2 and 3
equipment shipment due to bottlenecks with CPO production, alongside limited
additional growth drivers to be picked up in 2028 such as MEMS probe card and mass

reflow tools. This leads to weaker YoY sales growth of 67%/50% in 2027/28E and
operating margin of 29.3%/32.0% in 2027/28E as equipment shipment fails to scale.
This scenario implies a 14x 2028E PE, as valuation de-rate with dimmed outlook without
CPO and limited upside from thermal control module shipments.

Company Description
Company Description
Hermes Testing Solutions (HTSI) was founded in 2004 in Hsinchu,
Taiwan. It focuses on providing semiconductor testing and
inspecting solutions, equipment installation, technical support,
and maintenance services. Its main business segments are
customized semiconductor equipment, engineering services, and
probe cards and other materials.

Industry Outlook
New iterations of semiconductors, especially for AI / HPC
applications, are set to increase in both front-end wafer
fabrication advancements and back-end packaging complexities
with each iterations. With the industry now moves to utilize
"shift left", chip probing and its relevant supply chain will be key
beneficiaries of this trend which we believe will unlock multi-
year, secular growth trends.

Company Description
Hermes Testing Solutions (HTSI) was founded in 2004 in Hsinchu, Taiwan. It focuses on
providing semiconductor testing and inspecting solutions, equipment installation, technical
support, and maintenance services. Its main business segments are customized

semiconductor equipment, engineering services, and probe cards and other materials.
Valuation Method and Risk Statement
We value Hermes Testing based on PE methodology.
Risks for the semis industry:
- Geopolitical uncertainties: Geopolitical uncertainties including tariffs and more export
control could negatively impact semis cycle, and a downturn would lead to slower demand in
the overall market and moderating capacity expansion.
- Meaningful deceleration of new technology development: Slower demand and less

investment are expected If there is meaningful deceleration of new technology development
such as Cloud AI, autonomous driving, etc.
- Rising utility cost: Larger-than-expected utility cost increases would further pressure
profitability. Rising utility costs in Taiwan have negatively impacted margins for Taiwan tech
companies.
Company risks:
- Faster- or slower-than-expected front-end/CoWoS capacity expansion: This could result in
Hermes Testing gaining/losing equipment orders from major foundries and OSATs.

- Competitors achieving technological advancement: Potential risk of competition (from
either local or foreign companies) achieving technological advancements could result in
Hermes Testing losing market share from major foundries and OSATs.
- Better- or worse-than-expected AI growth: Better- or worse-than-expected AI growth could
lead to a ramp-up or a slowdown of equipment orders from major foundries and OSATs to
Hermes Testing, affecting long-term revenue growth potential.

- Slowdown or delay of new technological advancement: A potential slowdown or delay of
the rollout for technological advancements for technologies such as SoIC, CoPoS, and CPO,
would limit future equipment orders.
- Increase of raw material costs: Rising raw material costs may directly impact Hermes
Testing’s margins for equipment shipments.

KEY DEFINITIONS:Forecast Stock Return (FSR) is defined as expected percentage price appreciation plus gross dividend yield over
the next 12 months. In some cases, this yield may be based on accrued dividends. Market Return Assumption (MRA) is defined as
the one-year local market interest rate plus 5% (a proxy for, and not a forecast of, the equity risk premium). Under Review (UR)
Stocks may be flagged as UR by the analyst, indicating that the stock's price target and/or rating are subject to possible change in the

near term, usually in response to an event that may affect the investment case or valuation. Equity Price Targets have an investment
horizon of 12 months.

Investing in structured investments involves significant risks. For a detailed discussion of the risks involved in investing in any particular structured investment, you must read

the relevant offering materials for that investment. Structured investments are unsecured obligations of a particular issuer with returns linked to the performance of an
underlying asset. Depending on the terms of the investment, investors could lose all or a substantial portion of their investment based on the performance of the underlying
asset. Investors could also lose their entire investment if the issuer becomes insolvent. UBS does not guarantee in any way the obligations or the financial condition of any

issuer or the accuracy of any financial information provided by any issuer. Structured investments are not traditional investments and investing in a structured investment is
not equivalent to investing directly in the underlying asset. Structured investments may have limited or no liquidity, and investors should be prepared to hold their investment
to maturity. The return of structured investments may be limited by a maximum gain, participation rate or other feature. Structured investments may include call features and,

if a structured investment is called early, investors would not earn any further return and may not be able to reinvest in similar investments with similar terms. Structured
investments include costs and fees which are generally embedded in the price of the investment. The tax treatment of a structured investment may be complex and may differ
from a direct investment in the underlying asset. UBS and its employees do not provide tax advice. Investors should consult their own tax advisor about their own tax situation

before investing in any securities.

证券投资咨询业务证书编号：S1300516090001

9 月通胀点评

四季度通胀增速或走平   
9 月国际大宗商品价格波动对国内通胀的影响依然较大，国际油价、有色金
属价格造成明显上拉， 黄金价格则造成一定拖累；9 月 AI 产品价格上升对通
胀的影响进一步凸显，CPI 中通信工具分项同比增速连续两个月在 10%上方；
我们认为四季度 CPI 和 PPI 同比增速或在当前增速水平保持窄幅波动， 关注
国际局势、厄尔尼诺、生产端年底补库存等因素对通胀的扰动 。 
 9 月 CPI 环比增长 0.2%，同比增长 0.9%，核心 CPI 同比增长 1.0%，服务
价格同比增长 0.8%，消费品价格同比增长 1.0%。从分项同比看，同比增
速较 8 月上升的仅有交通通信，居住、教育文化娱乐和医疗保健增速持
平于 8 月，衣着、食品烟酒、生活用品及服务、其他用品和服务增速均
较 8 月有不同程度下降。 
 从环比看，9 月食品价格环比上涨 0.4%，涨幅与上月相同，低于季节性
水平 0.8 个百分点，影响 CPI 环比上涨约 0.06 个百分点，服务价格由上
月上涨 0.1%转为下降 0.2%，对 CPI 环比的影响由上月上拉约 0.04 个百
分点转为下拉约 0.08 个百分点 ， 工业消费品价格影响CPI 环比上涨约 0.22
个百分点。 
 AI 产品价格带动 9 月 CPI 同比增速上行。从同比看，9 月 CPI 同比增长
0.9%，其中，食品价格影响 CPI 同比下降约 0.29 个百分点，汽油价格影
响 CPI 同比上涨约 0.47 个百分点，扣除能源的工业消费品价格影响 CPI
同比上涨约 0.39 个百分点 ， 服务价格影响CPI 同比上涨约 0.40 个百分点 ；
从细项看，猪肉和鲜菜价格合计影响 CPI 同比下降约 0.33 个百分点，牛
肉和羊肉价格合计影响 CPI 同比上涨约 0.08 个百分点 ， 鸡蛋价格影响CPI
同比上涨约 0.07 个百分点，黄金饰品价格影响 CPI 同比上涨约 0.11 个百
分点，数据存储设备、平板电脑、计算机和移动电话机价格合计影响CPI
同比上涨约 0.23 个百分点。一方面，9 月 CPI 有三个特点与此前是一致
的，一是食品价格的持续拖累，二是国际能源价格和黄金价格的持续扰
动，三是内需的疲弱反映在服务价格上；另一方面，9 月也出现了新的特
点，最为明显的是 AI 产品需求带动的价格上升是 9 月 CPI 同比增速较 8
月上升的重要因素 ， 体现在通信工具价格9 月同比增长 14.3%、 交通通信
项目 9 月同比增长 4.3%，其次是以牛肉和羊肉为代表的食品价格，出现
环比走势强于季节性的趋势。我们认为四季度 CPI 同比增速或仍将维持
相对平稳的走势，但是需要开始关注 AI 产品需求对价格的推动、极端天
气对食品价格的影响、国际局势不确定性对大宗商品价格波动的影响，
以及 PPI 同比增速上升对 CPI 的外溢性影响。 
 9 月 PPI 环比增长 1.2%，同比增长 5.0%，其中生产资料同比增长 6.5%，
生活资料同比下降 0.1%。PPIRM 环比增长 1.2%，同比增长 6.9% 
 关注年底补库存对 PPI 的影响。9 月 PPI 同比增长 5.0%，涨幅明显高于
万得一致预期 4.3%，影响较大的主要是生产资料中采掘业和原材料工业
价格，从行业看主要的上行拉动，一是石油、有色、化工等受输入性因
素影响较大的行业，二是受 AI 需求带动较大的计算机通信行业，三是受
季节性因素影响较大的行业，比如煤炭。我们认为四季度国际局势的不
确定性和国内生产端季节性补库存的具体情况 ， 都可能对PPI 价格造成影
响 ， 但考虑到目前国内供强虚弱的情况没有明显变化 ， 我们认为PPI 同比
增速短期虽然回落空间不大，但进一步上升的空间也较小 。 
 风险提示：全球通胀二次上行的风险 ；欧美经济回落速度 偏快；国际局
势复杂化。

### Page 2

2026 年 10 月 14 日 9 月通胀点评 2

CPI 
9 月 CPI 环比增速较 8 月回落。9 月 CPI 环比增长 0.2%，增速较 8 月下降 0.2 个百分点。从分项环
比看，9 月环比上行的包括交通通信 （1.0%） 、 衣着 （0.6%） 、 食品烟酒 （0.2%） 、 医疗保健 （0.2%），
环比增速下降的有生活用品及服务 （-0.1%） 、 教育文化娱乐 （-0.3%） 、 其他用品和服务 （-0.8%），
居住价格环比增速持平；9 月分项中较 8 月环比增速上升的有衣着、医疗保健、生活用品及服务，
较 8 月环比增速下降的有食品烟酒、教育文化娱乐、交通通信、其他用品和服务，居住环比增速持
平于 8 月。 
9 月 CPI 同比增速略低于万得一致预期。9 月 CPI 同比增长 0.9%，较 8 月上升 0.1 个百分点，核心
CPI 同比增长 1.0%，较8 月持平 ， 服务价格同比增长0.8%，较8 月持平 ， 消费品价格同比增长1.0%，
较 8 月上升 0.2 个百分点。从分项同比看，9 月同比增速增长的是其他用品和服务（5.9%） 、交通通
信（4.3%） 、医疗保健 （2.7%） 、教育文化娱乐 （1.4%） 、衣着 （1.2%） 、生活用品及服务 （0.2%），
同比增速下降的是居住（-0.3%）和食品烟酒（-0.9%）；同比增速较 8 月上升的仅有交通通信，居
住、教育文化娱乐和医疗保健增速持平于 8 月，衣着、食品烟酒、生活用品及服务、其他用品和服
务增速均较 8 月有不同程度下降。 
9 月食品价格分化，牛羊肉价格环比涨幅扩大。从环比看，9 月食品价格环比上涨 0.4%，涨幅与上
月相同，低于季节性水平 0.8 个百分点，影响 CPI 环比上涨约 0.06 个百分点；鲜菜、猪肉、鸡蛋和
鲜果价格涨幅在 0.5%-2.3%之间，合计影响 CPI 环比上涨约 0.05 个百分点；牛肉和羊肉价格持续上
涨，本月分别上涨 3.1%和 1.4%，涨幅均有扩大，合计影响 CPI 环比上涨约 0.04 个百分点；休渔期
结束，水产品供应充足，价格下降 0.8%，影响 CPI 环比下降约 0.02 个百分点。从同比看，食品价格
同比下降 1.7%，降幅比上月扩大 0.3 个百分点，影响 CPI 同比下降约 0.29 个百分点；食品中，猪肉
和鲜菜价格分别下降 10.0%和 8.1%，合计影响 CPI 同比下降约 0.33 个百分点；粮食、食用油、奶类
和鲜果价格降幅在 0.6%-1.5%之间 ， 合计影响CPI 同比下降约 0.07 个百分点 ； 鸡蛋价格上涨17.4%，
涨幅回落 1.1 个百分点 ， 影响CPI 同比上涨约 0.07 个百分点 ； 牛肉和羊肉价格分别上涨 7.5%和 6.4%，
涨幅继续扩大，合计影响 CPI 同比上涨约 0.08 个百分点。 
服务价格和黄金价格环比回落 ，AI 产品价格同比涨幅扩大。从环比看 ，9 月服务价格由上月上涨 0.1%
转为下降 0.2%，对CPI 环比的影响由上月上拉约 0.04 个百分点转为下拉约 0.08 个百分点 ， 服务中，
随着暑假结束，飞机票、宾馆住宿和旅行社收费价格合计影响 CPI 环比下降约 0.12 个百分点；秋季
开学，教育服务价格影响 CPI 环比上涨约 0.03 个百分点；工业消费品价格影响 CPI 环比上涨约 0.22
个百分点，其中，受国际市场价格变动影响，国内汽油价格和黄金饰品价格两项合计影响 CPI 环比
上涨约 0.14 个百分点；受成本走高影响，移动电话机、计算机、数据存储设备和平板电脑价格合计
影响 CPI 环比上涨约 0.04 个百分点 ； 秋装上市 ， 带动服装价格影响CPI 环比上涨约 0.03 个百分点。
从同比看，9 月汽油价格影响 CPI 同比上涨约 0.47 个百分点；扣除能源的工业消费品价格影响 CPI
同比上涨约 0.39 个百分点 ； 其中 ， 黄金饰品价格影响CPI 同比上涨约 0.11 个百分点 ， 数据存储设备、
平板电脑、计算机和移动电话机价格合计影响 CPI 同比上涨约 0.23 个百分点；服务价格涨幅与上月
相同，影响 CPI 同比上涨约 0.40 个百分点。 
AI 产品价格带动 9 月 CPI 同比增速上行。从同比看，9 月 CPI 同比增长 0.9%，其中，食品价格影
响 CPI 同比下降约 0.29 个百分点 ， 汽油价格影响CPI 同比上涨约 0.47 个百分点 ， 扣除能源的工业消
费品价格影响 CPI 同比上涨约 0.39 个百分点 ， 服务价格影响CPI 同比上涨约 0.40 个百分点 ； 从细项
看，猪肉和鲜菜价格合计影响 CPI 同比下降约 0.33 个百分点，牛肉和羊肉价格合计影响 CPI 同比上
涨约 0.08 个百分点 ， 鸡蛋价格影响CPI 同比上涨约 0.07 个百分点 ， 黄金饰品价格影响CPI 同比上涨
约 0.11 个百分点 ， 数据存储设备 、 平板电脑 、 计算机和移动电话机价格合计影响CPI 同比上涨约 0.23
个百分点。一方面，9 月 CPI 有三个特点与此前是一致的，一是食品价格的持续拖累，二是国际能
源价格和黄金价格的持续扰动，三是内需的疲弱反映在服务价格上；另一方面， 9 月也出现了新的
特点 ， 最为明显的是AI 产品需求带动的价格上升是 9 月 CPI 同比增速较 8 月上升的重要因素 ， 体现
在通信工具价格 9 月同比增长 14.3%、交通通信项目 9 月同比增长 4.3%，其次是以牛肉和羊肉为代
表的食品价格，出现环比走势强于季节性的趋势。我们认为四季度 CPI 同比增速或仍将维持相对平
稳的走势，但是需要开始关注 AI 产品需求对价格的推动、极端天气对食品价格的影响、国际局势不
确定性对大宗商品价格波动的影响，以及 PPI 同比增速上升对 CPI 的外溢性影响。

### Page 4

2026 年 10 月 14 日 9 月通胀点评 4

PPI 
PPI 环比大幅走高。9 月 PPI 环比增长 1.2%，较8 月上升 0.8 个百分点 ， 其中生产资料环比增长1.4%，
生活资料环比增长 0.1%。9 月 PPI 同比增长 5.0%，较 8 月上升 1.2 个百分点，其中生产资料同比增
长 6.5%，较 8 月上升 1.5 个百分点，生活资料同比下降 0.1%，较 8 月上升 0.4 个百分点。 
9 月 PPIRM 环比增长 1.2%，同比增长 6.9%，较 8 月上升 1.1 个百分点；从构成来看，9 月同比增速
上升较多的是有色金属材料类（ 20.0%），燃料、动力类（ 13.6%）和化工原料类（ 12.6%），同比
增速下降的仅有建筑材料类（-2.3%），农副产品类（-0.4%）和黑色金属材料类（-0.4%）；所有品
类中，同比增速较 8 月上升较多的是燃料、动力类，化工原料类和建筑材料类，同比增速下降的仅
有黑色金属材料类。 
9 月主要行业中出厂价格同比增速较高的是，煤炭开采和洗选业（ 35.3%），石油和天然气开采业
（24.5%）和石油、煤炭及其他燃料加工业（21.6%），同比增速较低的是酒、饮料和精制茶制造业
（-5.3%），电力、热力的生产和供应业（-3.9%）和黑色金属矿采选业（-3.4%）；同比增速较 8 月
上升较多的是石油和天然气开采业，石油、煤炭及其他燃料加工业和煤炭开采和洗选业，下降的仅
有黑色金属矿采选业，造纸及纸制品业，电力、热力的生产和供应业以及木材加工及木、竹、藤、
棕、草制品业。 
输入性因素和季节性因素对 PPI 同比增速影响较大。9 月 PPI 环比运行的主要特点 ：一是国际原油、
有色金属价格上行带动国内相关行业价格上涨 。 石油开采 、 精炼石油产品制造 、 有机化学原料制造、
有色金属矿采选业 、 有色金属冶炼和压延加工业 ，5 个行业合计影响 PPI 环比上涨约 0.63 个百分点。
二是部分能源和原材料行业供需状况变化拉动价格上涨。受冬季供暖备煤需求增加、主产矿区供应
阶段性偏紧、国际煤价上涨预期效应等因素影响，煤炭开采和洗选业、煤炭加工价格环比上涨，钢
材、水泥等建筑材料需求有所回升，加之原材料成本上涨，非金属矿物制品业价格在环比连降 7 个
月后转为上涨，黑色金属冶炼和压延加工业价格环比上涨，4 个行业合计影响 PPI 环比上涨约 0.38
个百分点。三是人工智能、算电协同等领域加快发展带动部分行业 需求增加、价格上涨。电气机械
和器材制造业 、 计算机通信和其他电子设备制造业2 个行业合计影响 PPI 环比上涨约 0.07 个百分点。
从同比看，价格上涨的行业中，煤炭开采和洗选业、石油和天然气开采业、石油煤炭及其他燃料加
工业、化学原料和化学制品制造业、有色金属矿采选业、有色金属冶炼和压延加工业、电气机械和
器材制造业、计算机通信和其他电子设备制造业，8 个行业涨幅比上月均扩大，合计影响 PPI 同比
上涨约 5.42 个百分点，上拉影响比上月增加 1.12 个百分点，价格下降的行业中，酒饮料和精制茶制
造业、电力热力生产和供应业、医药制造 业、非金属矿物制品业、汽车制造业， 5 个行业合计下拉
PPI 同比约 0.66 个百分点。 
关注年底补库存对 PPI 的影响。9 月 PPI 同比增长 5.0%，涨幅明显高于万得一致预期 4.3%，影响较
大的主要是生产资料中采掘业和原材料工业价格，从行业看主要 的上行拉动，一是石油、有色、化
工等受输入性因素影响较大的行业 ，二是受AI 需求带动较大的计算机通信行业 ， 三是受季节性因素
影响较大的行业，比如煤炭。我们认为四季度国际局势的不确定性和国内生产端季节性补库存的具
体情况，都可能对 PPI 价格造成影响，但考虑到目前国内供强虚弱的情况没有明显变化，我们认为
PPI 同比增速短期虽然回落空间不大，但进一步上升的空间也较小 。

披露声明 
本报告准确表述了证券分析师的个人观点。该证券分析师声明，本人未在公司内、外部机构兼任有损本人独立性与客
观性的其他职务，没有担任本报告评论的上市公司的董事、监事或高级管理人员；也不拥有与该上市公司有关的任何
财务权益；本报告评论的上市公司或其它第三方都没有或没有承诺向本人提供与本报告有关的任何补偿或其它利益。  
中银国际证券股份有限公司同时声明，将通过公司网站披露本公司授权公众媒体及其他机构刊载或者转发证券研究报
告有关情况 。 如有投资者于未经授权的公众媒体看到或从其他机构获得本研究报告的 ， 请慎重使用所获得的研究报告，

评级体系说明 
以报告发布日后公司股价/行业指数涨跌幅相对同期相关市场指数的涨跌幅的表现为基准： 
公司投资评级： 
买    入：预计该公司股价在未来 6-12 个月内超越基准指数 20%以上； 
增    持：预计该公司股价在未来 6-12 个月内超越基准指数 10%-20%； 
中    性：预计该公司股价在未来 6-12 个月内相对基准指数变动幅度在-10%-10%之间； 
减    持：预计该公司股价在未来 6-12 个月内相对基准指数跌幅在 10%以上； 
未有评级：因无法获取必要的资料或者其他原因，未能给出明确的投资评级。

行业投资评级： 
强于大市：预计该行业指数在未来 6-12 个月内表现强于基准指数； 
中    性：预计该行业指数在未来 6-12 个月内表现基本与基准指数持平； 
弱于大市：预计该行业指数在未来 6-12 个月内表现弱于基准指数； 
未有评级：因无法获取必要的资料或者其他原因，未能给出明确的投资评级。

沪深市场基准指数为沪深 300 指数；新三板市场基准指数为三板成指或三板做市指数；香港市场基准指数为恒生指数
或恒生中国企业指数；美股市场基准指数为纳斯达 克综合指数或标普 500 指数。

### Page 1

2026.09.21   
LG에너지솔루션 
(373220)

원-달러 환율 하락에도 실적 개선세 지속 Company Brief

3Q26 원/달러 환율 하락에도 불구하고 영업이익 시장 기대치 상회 전망 
동사의 3Q26 실적은 매출 8.8조원(+45% YoY, + 16% QoQ), 영업이익 3,690억원(-
39% YoY, +226% QoQ)을 기록하며 원/달러 환율 하락으로 매출은 시장 기대치를 
소폭 하회하겠으나 영업이익은 상회할 것으로 전망된다. AMPC 는 미국 ESS 생산량 
증가와 GM 얼티엄셀즈 재가동에 힘입어 전분기 대비 약 57% 증가한 3,790억원으로 
추정된다. 미국 상호관세 위법 판결에 따른 관세 환급금이나 완성차 업체들의 최소 
구매 물량 미달 보상금 반영 여부에 따라 추가적인 이익 개선 가능성도 존재한다. ESS 
부문은 전분기에서 이연된 물량의 출하가 본격화되면서 3Q26 매출이 전분기 대비 약 
38% 증가할 것으로 예상된다. 특히 미국 ESS 생산라인의 가동 안정화와 AMPC 수혜 
확대에 힘입어 수익성도 점진적으로 개선될 것으로 보인다. 자동차전지 부문도 
3Q26부터 회복세가 강화될 것으로 예상된다. 유럽향 물량 회복과 GM 얼티엄셀즈 
재가동으로 매출이 전분기 대비 큰 폭으로 증가하고, 고정비 부담 완화로 적자폭도 
뚜렷하게 축소될 전망이다. 소형전지 부문은 주요 고객사인 Tesla의 판매 호조에 따른 
안정적인 출하 증가로 매출 회복과 수익성 방어가 가능할 것으로 예상된다. 
4Q26 대규모 ESS 신규 수주 기대 
전세계 ESS 시장은 신재생 에너지 발전 확대와 AIDC 투자 본격화에 힘입어 빠르게 
성장하고 있다. 시장조사기관 SNE리서치에 따르면 1H26 전세계 ESS 배터리 
출하량은 461GWh로 전년 동기 대비 약 71% 증가했다. 이 중 북미 출하량은 
76GWh로 전년 동기 대비 약 83% 증가했으며, 분기별로는 1Q26 35GWh(+91% 
YoY), 2Q26 41GWh(+76% YoY) 를 기록했다. 통상 하반기에 출하가 집중되는 
계절성을 감안하면 올해 북미 ESS 배터리 출하량은 전년 대비 약 70% 증가한 
170~180GWh에 이를 것으로 전망된다. 연초 동사가 예상한 올해 북미 ESS 수요 
성장률 10% 초반 수준을 크게 상회할 가능성이 높은 상황이다. 올해 동사의 ESS 
신규 수주 목표는 약 90GWh이며, 현재까지 약 30GWh의 수주를 확정한 것으로 
파악된다. 이에 시장 일각에서는 연간 수주 목표 달성 여부에 대한 우려가 제기되고 
있다. 그러나 당사가 파악한 바에 따르면 대규모 ESS 신규 수주가 4Q26에 집중될 
것으로 예상된다. 특히 북미 ESS 수요의 가파른 증가세와 투자세액공제(48E)에 
연계된 PFE 공급망 규제 시행은 동사의 수주 확보에 우호적으로 작용할 전망이다. 
매수 투자의견과 목표주가 유지 
동사에 대한 매수 투자의견과 목표주가를 유지한다. 향후 국내 배터리 셀 업체들의 
북미 ESS 신규 수주가 지속적으로 확대될 전망이다. 미국 ESS 시장에서는 신재생 
에너지 발전 확대와 AIDC 투자 본격화에 따른 수요 증가, 중국 공급망에 대한 규제 
강화가 맞물리면서 국내 업체들에 우호적인 사업 환경이 조성되고 있다. 동사 역시 
ESS 중심의 생산라인 전환과 가동률 상승이 본격화될 경우 매출 성장과 함께 고정비 
부담 완화, AMPC 수혜 확대에 따른 수익성 개선이 기대된다. 이를 바탕으로 동사의 
영업이익은 2026년 약 0.8조원에서 2028년 약 5.1조원으로 가파르게 증가할 것으로 
전망된다. ESS의 역할이 신재생 에너지 연계용 저장장치를 넘어 AIDC의 안정적인 
전력 공급을 지원하는 핵심 인프라로 확대되고 있다는 점에도 주목할 필요가 있다. 
이에 따라 동사의 밸류에이션 역시 기존 EV 중심의 평가에서 벗어나 전력 인프라 
산업의 성장성을 반영하는 방향으로 재평가되어야 한다고 판단된다. 업종 내에서 실적 
개선 가시성과 정책 수혜가 뚜렷한 동사에 대한 비중 확대 전략이 유효하다.

Document 154: AI Scare Raises Growth Fears and Lowers Bond Yields

Source PDF: all report pdf/AI Scare Raises Growth Fears and Lowers Bond Yields.pdf
Report source: researchmail轉寄
Date: 2026-10-16
Pages: 8
Status: ok

### Page 1

From:    Yardeni QuickTakes <yardeni-research@ghost.io>
Subject: AI Scare Raises Growth Fears and Lowers Bond Yields
Date:    4 hours ago
AI Scare Raises Growth Fears and Lowers Bond Yields

YARDENI QUICKTAKES
AI Scare Raises Growth Fears
and Lowers Bond Yields
By Ed Yardeni & Elias Griepentrog • 15 Oct 2026
View in browser
Photo by Jonny Gios / Unsplash

I. Treasury Yields
US Treasury yields fell sharply today, extending Wednesday's retreat from
intraday highs. The 10-year yield dropped 9bps to 5.10%, while the 30-
year fell 10bps to 5.43% (chart).

### Page 2

Even the 2-year yield declined slightly to 4.77% (chart).
Remarkably, this broad-based decline occurred despite two hotter-than-
expected inflation reports. Normally, such surprises would push Treasury
yields higher as investors priced in a more hawkish Fed.
So, what's going on? We think the bond market is focusing on the
sustainability of the AI investment boom. That issue came to the fore this
week as CoreWeave's bonds plunged below 80 cents on the dollar and

debt financing major data center projects also tumbled. A new Gartner
report estimates AI data center operators will need $8.5 trillion in revenues
through 2030 to cover costs and earn healthy returns, versus projected
revenues of "just" $3.6 trillion.

### Page 3

Why does this matter? The AI buildout has become a major engine of US
economic growth. But doubts about future returns are pushing up
borrowing costs, potentially constraining access to capital and ultimately
slowing investment spending.
As a result, investors may be lowering their economic growth expectations
amid concerns that the AI boom could slow, pushing Treasury yields lower.
This reinforces our longstanding view that the earlier surge in Treasury

yields largely reflected stronger-than-expected nominal GDP growth
(chart).
We are nowhere near ready to write off the AI investment boom. Demand
for compute continues to outpace supply, while rising adoption should fuel
further investment. Indeed, just five hyperscalers (Amazon, Alphabet,
Meta, Microsoft, and Oracle) are expected to spend a combined $892
billion this year and $1.1 trillion next year. Their strong balance sheets and
cash flow generation make their spending plans relatively insensitive to

higher borrowing costs. While heavily indebted AI infrastructure providers
may struggle, we expect the hyperscalers to keep spending and the
Roaring 2020s to keep roaring.
II. Inflation
Let's take a closer look at September's hotter-than-expected PPI report
and what it tells us about inflationary pressures in the pipeline.

### Page 4

(1) PPI for final demand. September's PPI final demand surged 1.3%
m/m, largely reflecting a 10.8% spike in energy prices. However, even
excluding the volatile food, energy, and trade services components,
producer prices still rose 0.5%, suggesting that inflationary pressures
remain broad-based.
On a y/y basis, core PPI final demand rose to 5.0%, with services and
goods prices rising 5.0% and 5.2%, respectively (chart).
(2) PPI for consumption. The PPI for personal consumption, a key input

into the Fed’s preferred PCED inflation gauge, surged by 1.3% m/m in
September (chart). Excluding food and energy, it was up 0.6%.

### Page 5

Core PPI personal consumption rose to 4.6% y/y and remained well
above both core PCED and core CPI inflation, suggesting that upside
risks to consumer inflation remain elevated (chart).
(3) Supercore inflation. The PPI supercore inflation rate rose to 4.6% y/y
in September (chart).

### Page 6

(4) Core PCED. With September CPI and PPI now in hand, most
forecasters expect core PCED inflation to rise 0.3% m/m in September,
which would lift the y/y rate from 3.0% to 3.1%. The Cleveland Fed’s
Inflation Nowcasting model projects 0.28% m/m and 3.05% y/y,
respectively.
III. Booming Economy
For now, the US economy shows no signs of slowing down. Consider the
latest data points:
(1) Jobless claims. Initial jobless claims edged up slightly to 199,000 in

the week ended October 10, the fifth straight week below 200,000. That is
the longest streak since 1969! Continuing claims declined to 1.71 million,
dragging down the 4-week moving average to the lowest level since April
2023 (chart).

### Page 7

(2) Retail sales. September retail sales rose 0.8% m/m, partly driven by a
6.4% surge in gasoline station sales amid elevated pump prices (chart).
Still, eight of the 13 major categories posted gains. Sales at food services
and drinking places, the only services category and a key discretionary
spending indicator, jumped 1.1% m/m, their eighth consecutive monthly
gain. They rose 7.0% y/y, the strongest increase since March 2023.
(3) GDP. Reflecting today's retail sales report, the Atlanta Fed’s GDPNow

model estimates Q3 real GDP growth at 3.6% (saar), unchanged from
October 8 (chart). Consumer spending is projected to rise 3.6%. AI-related
demand is boosting business equipment investment (17.6%), fixed
business structures (7.0%), and intellectual property investment (6.6%).
Final sales to private domestic purchasers are expected to grow 4.7%.

### Page 8

clause.

Document 175: Anthropic估值预期承压：收入增速放缓，部分投资者转向1.5万亿美元

Source PDF: all report pdf/Anthropic估值预期承压：收入增速放缓，部分投资者转向1.5万亿美元.pdf
Report source: WallstreetCN (Global)
Date: 2026-10-15
Pages: 2
Status: ok

### Page 1

据媒体报道， Anthropic Q3 营收环⽐增⻓ 45% ，远不及 Q2 逾 140% 的增速，部分投资者认
为，承销商应下调 IPO 估值预期，估值可能低于 1.5 万亿美元，⽽此前部分投⾏私下讨论的
⽔平约为 2 万亿美元。 Jefferies 分析师表⽰，华尔街可能预期 Anthropic 的年化收⼊⾄少达
到 800 亿美元，并希望年底前超过 1050 亿美元。
摘要：
Anthropic 第三季度收⼊继续⾼速增⻓，但增速明显放缓，令部分 IPO 潜在投资者开始重新审视
这家 AI 公司的估值预期。在 AI 交易承压、融资成本上升的背景下，市场对⾼增⻓能否⽀撑⾼估值
的疑虑正在加深。
据《 The Information 》报道， Anthropic 在周三举⾏的旧⾦⼭投资者会议上披露，第三季度收⼊
达到 168 亿美元，环⽐增⻓约 45% ，低于第⼆季度超过 140% 的增幅。
部分潜在投资者认为，承销商应下调 IPO 估值预期，估值可能低于 1.5 万亿美元，⽽此前部分投

⾏私下讨论的⽔平约为 2 万亿美元。
⾏业竞争加剧， OpenAI 正在加强企业级 AI 产品，并通过更低价格竞争； Google 、 Meta 等公司的
新模型，以及开源模型，也在给 Anthropic 带来压⼒。
Anthropic 增⻓放缓为已经⾯临通胀和利率上⾏压⼒的 AI 交易再添不确定性。市场不仅关注 AI 公
司的收⼊规模，也开始审视增⻓预期能否兑现，以及巨额芯⽚和基础设施投⼊能否获得相应回
报。
收⼊增速明显放缓，增⻓预期⾯临考验
Anthropic 第三季度收⼊从第⼆季度的 116 亿美元增⾄ 168 亿美元，较第⼀季度的 47 亿美元继续⼤
幅增⻓。但推动公司此前收⼊快速攀升的动⼒正在发⽣变化。
第⼆季度收⼊环⽐增⻓超过 140% ，主要受 Claude Code 需求激增推动；第三季度环⽐增速则降
⾄约 45% 。尽管收⼊仍在快速扩张，但增速降档可能影响投资者对公司未来增⻓路径的判断。
收⼊结构也存在变化。 Anthropic 估计，模型蒸馏相关收⼊约占总收⼊的 5% ⾄ 7% ，公司预计这

部分收⼊将在当前季度下降，并继续拖累收⼊表现。模型蒸馏是指其他公司利⽤现有 AI 模型的输
出训练⾃⼰的模型。
Anthropic 已连续两个季度实现调整后盈利，三季度现⾦流也转为正值。公司披露的年内调整后
营业利润率为 11% ，但这⼀指标不包括股票薪酬等重⼤成本。
部分投资者重新评估 IPO 估值
收⼊增速放缓正对 Anthropic 的 IPO 估值预期构成压⼒。
部分潜在投资者对最新财务数据感到失望，认为承销商需要降低估值预期，部分投资者讨论的
估值⽔平可能低于 1.5 万亿美元，低于此前部分投⾏私下提出的约 2 万亿美元。
这⼀变化反映的是投资者对估值的重新讨论，并不意味着 Anthropic 已经正式下调 IPO 定价。对
于潜在投资者⽽⾔，关键问题在于，公司能否持续实现⾜够快的收⼊增⻓，以⽀撑此前的⾼估

Jefferies 分析师 Jeffrey Favuzza 表⽰，华尔街可能预期 Anthropic 的年化收⼊运⾏率⾄少达到
800 亿美元，并希望年底前超过 1050 亿美元。市场对增⻓的要求已经很⾼，因此，即使收⼊继续
增⻓，若未达到预期，也可能引发估值压⼒。
巨额基础设施投⼊加剧回报压⼒
收 ⼊ 增 ⻓ 与 盈 利改 善 之 外 ， Anthropic 还 需 要 应 对 庞 ⼤ 的 基 础 设施 投 ⼊ 。 媒 体 此 前 报 道 ，

如此规模的投⼊，使收⼊增⻓能否持续转化为现⾦流和利润成为估值讨论的重要部分。在融资
成本上升的环境下，投资者也更关注 AI 企业能否以合理的成本扩⼤业务，⽽⾮仅仅追求收⼊规

模。
Anthropic 财务披露引发市场关注之际， AI 相关资产已经⾯临更⼴泛的压⼒。投资者对 AI 云服务
商 CoreWeave 的财务状况存在担忧，韩国存储芯⽚股也出现⼤幅下跌。与此同时，市场还在消
化通胀数据偏热及利率可能维持⾼位的⻛险。
这些因素并不意味着 AI 需求正在全⾯减弱，但它们正在改变市场评估 AI 企业的⽅式。对于需要持
续投⼊⼤量资⾦的公司⽽⾔，融资成本、收⼊增⻓和投资回报之间的关系正受到更严格的审
视。

Document 180: ASML强劲财报也救不了！美芯片股跌势加剧，行业指数市值蒸发万亿美元

Source PDF: all report pdf/ASML强劲财报也救不了！美芯片股跌势加剧，行业指数市值蒸发万亿美元.pdf
Report source: Unknown
Date: 未知日期
Pages: 4
Status: ok

### Page 1

费城半导体指数周三收跌超 4% ，六连跌、创近 17 个⽉最⻓连跌，过去六⽇，该指数过半成
分股⾄少累跌 10% 。 ASML 处于半导体产业链上游，其光刻设备需求旺盛，能说明芯⽚制造
商仍在规划产能扩张，却不能直接证明芯⽚制造商的下游客⼾会持续以同样的速度购买芯
⽚。虽然 AI ⾏情尚未被证伪，但市场正在提⾼回报⻔槛。
摘要：
周⼆盘中刚刚缓和的芯⽚股抛售潮周三再度加剧，美光科技、 SK 海⼒⼠等存储芯⽚股重新⾛
弱，⾏业指标费城半导体指数（ SOX ）到收盘跌超 4% ，连续第六个交易⽇收跌。
媒体指出，随着利率上升、估值偏⾼和 AI 基础设施建设成本⾼企等担忧不断累积， SOX 成分股
的总市值⼀周来蒸发超过 1 万亿美元。美东时间 14 ⽇周三盘中， SOX 跌幅超过 3% 时，该指数过
去六个交易⽇的累计跌幅已达到 10% ，超过⼀半的 SOX 成分股六⽇内跌幅⾄少达 10% 。
这轮调整正在从短期获利了结演变为对 AI 投资逻辑的全⾯检验。 9 ⽉美国 CPI 通胀强于预期，进

⼀步强化了市场对美联储可能继续加息的担忧；博通 CEO 陈福阳此前警告 AI ⾏业可能受到资⾦
约束；⽽即使半导体设备⻰头 ASML 交出强劲业绩、上调全年销售指引，也未能有效提振芯⽚
股。
市场真正追问的，已经不只是 AI 需求是否旺盛，⽽是巨额资本开⽀能否转化为可持续的盈利增
⻓，以及当前估值是否已经透⽀未来收益。
SOX ⾯临六连跌，全球芯⽚股市值蒸发⻛险逼近万亿美元
周三盘中，费城半导体指数刷新⽇低时跌幅接近 4.7% ，最终收跌 4.3% ，连跌六个交易⽇，连续
两⽇创 2025 年 5 ⽉以来最⻓连跌⽇。
个股层⾯，调整已从存储芯⽚扩散⾄芯⽚设计和制造设备领域。彭博数据显⽰，过去六个交易
⽇中，超过半数 SOX 成分股跌幅⾄少达到 10% 。 Arm 美股（ ARM ）同期累计下跌近 20% ，应⽤
材料（ AMAT ）下跌约 15% ，英特尔（ INTL ）累跌约 11% ， GlobalFoundries （ GFS ）则是同期
唯⼀累涨的成分股。
到周三收盘， Arm 跌 6.8% ，英特尔跌近 4.6% 。在周⼆盘中跌势有所缓和后，存储芯⽚股再度集

体⾛弱： SK 海⼒⼠美股（ SKHY ）跌超 6% ，闪迪（ SNDK ）跌超 5% ，希捷科技（ STX ）跌
4.5% ，美光科技（ MU ）和西部数据（ WDC ）跌超 4% 。

### Page 2

从韩国、⽇本到美国，存储芯⽚股此前已经历剧烈调整，如今跌势进⼀步扩展⾄芯⽚设计和设
备制造商，显⽰投资者正在降低对整个半导体板块的⻛险敞⼝。
彭博指出，按近期跌幅推算， SOX 成分股⾯临超过 1 万亿美元的市值损失⻛险。这并不意味着相
关市值已经全部蒸发，⽽是意味着若跌势持续，全球半导体⾏业可能⾯临规模惊⼈的估值回
撤。
通胀升温、加息担忧重燃， AI ⾼估值⾯临压⼒
推动芯⽚股继续下跌的因素之⼀，是宏观利率环境再次趋紧。
美国 9 ⽉通胀数据强于市场预期，消费者价格涨幅达到 4 ⽉以来最⾼⽔平，燃料成本上升是重要
推⼿。这⼀数据强化了市场对美联储后续可能进⼀步加息的预期，即使美联储在本⽉晚些时候
的会议上暂时按兵不动，投资者对未来利率路径的判断也已发⽣变化。
对于估值较⾼、盈利预期⾼度依赖未来增⻓的科技股⽽⾔，利率上升意味着未来现⾦流的折现
价值下降，也会提⾼企业融资和扩张的成本。半导体企业既受益于 AI 基础设施建设，⼜⾯临巨额
资本投⼊和较⻓的回报周期，因此对利率变化尤其敏感。
与此同时， AI 交易本⾝也在⾯临重新审视。博通 CEO 陈福阳本周早些时候将部分追逐 AI 热潮的

投资者⽐作赌场赌徒，并警告⾏业可能受到资⾦约束。这⼀表态进⼀步加剧了市场对 AI 投资热潮
可持续性的担忧。
过去⼏年，投资者愿意为算⼒需求快速增⻓⽀付⾼估值；如今，市场开始更严格地衡量企业的
融资能⼒、资本开⽀规模和实际盈利兑现速度。当通胀推⾼利率预期、资⾦成本上升与估值压
⼒同时出现时，即便⾏业⻓期需求仍然强劲，相关股票也可能经历剧烈调整。
ASML 业绩强劲，为何仍然救不了芯⽚股？

### Page 3

本周芯⽚股跌势加剧之际，半导体设备⻰头 ASML 公布了强劲的季度业绩，并上调全年销售指
引，客⼾对未来数年的设备需求也相当旺盛。然⽽，这份财报仍未能扭转整个芯⽚板块的跌
势。
《巴伦周刊》分析认为，关键在于 ASML 处于半导体产业链的上游。其光刻设备需求旺盛，能够
说明芯⽚制造商仍在规划产能扩张，却不能直接证明芯⽚制造商的下游客⼾会持续以同样的速
度购买芯⽚。
ASML 的客⼾包括台积电、英特尔等芯⽚制造商，⽽推动当前 AI 投资周期的关键终端客⼾，则是
微软、⾕歌、亚⻢逊等⼤型云服务商及其他超⼤规模数据中⼼运营商。对于芯⽚制造商⽽⾔，
设备订单和⻓期产能规划固然重要，但更直接影响收⼊和利润的，是下游客⼾能否持续采购芯
⽚，以及这些采购能否带来⾜够的回报。
ASML 强劲的业绩说明 AI 相关设备需求仍有韧性，但这并不能完全消除市场对 AI 资本开⽀效率的
担忧。换⾔之，设备商的订单能⻅度与芯⽚企业的盈利兑现，并不是同⼀回事。
此外， ASML 的强劲需求还可能带来另⼀重影响：如果设备供应紧张推动其提⾼产品价格，台积

电、英特尔等客⼾未来的设备采购成本也可能随之上升。对本就需要投⼊巨额资⾦建设先进制
程产能的企业⽽⾔，这可能进⼀步增加资本开⽀压⼒。
因此， ASML 财报未能提振整个芯⽚板块，并不必然意味着 AI 需求已经转弱，⽽是表明市场不再
满⾜于看到上游订单增⻓，还希望确认整条产业链都能从 AI 投资中获得可持续的盈利。
华尔街下调预期： AI 投资要从⽀出转向盈利
瑞银⾸席投资办公室的乌尔⾥克 · 霍夫曼 - 布尔查迪在周三的⼀份研究报告中指出，近期全球芯⽚
股⾯临的压⼒不断加⼤。她认为， AI 领域竞争加剧、融资需求上升和利率⾛⾼，都可能不时放⼤
市场波动，但本轮调整更像是对市场预期的重新校准，⽽⾮ AI 投资逻辑发⽣根本性断裂。
这⼀判断反映出市场当前的分歧：⼀⽅⾯， AI 基础设施建设仍可能持续多年；另⼀⽅⾯，投资者
开始质疑资本开⽀的增⻓速度是否可持续，以及由此产⽣的收益能否满⾜此前过⾼的预期。
彭博指出，市场接下来的⼀个重要观察窗⼝，是 Anthropic 据称本周三在旧⾦⼭举⾏的投资者活

动。华尔街希望从这家 AI 公司的披露中了解其收⼊运⾏速度，以便更准确地判断 AI 企业的收⼊增
⻓能否⽀撑持续扩⼤的算⼒⽀出。
瑞银建议投资者重点关注 AI ⽀出究竟能否转化为可持续的盈利增⻓，以及 AI 在企业和消费者端的
应⽤是否继续扩⼤。与单纯追踪资本开⽀规模相⽐，这些指标更有助于判断当前投资热潮能否
形成稳定的商业回报。
如果 AI 企业能够持续扩⼤收⼊、提⾼盈利能⼒，并证明巨额基础设施投资具有合理回报，那么当
前调整可能更多是估值和预期的重新校准。反之，如果⽀出增⻓快于收⼊和现⾦流改善，市场
对⾼估值的容忍度就可能进⼀步下降。
AI ⾏情尚未被证伪，但市场正在提⾼回报⻔槛
从连续下跌的 SOX ，到 ASML 强劲财报未能带来的板块反弹，再到通胀和利率预期重新施压，当
前芯⽚股⾏情显⽰，投资者正从追逐 AI 增⻓转向检验 AI 投资的实际经济回报。
这并不意味着 AI 需求已经崩塌。 ASML 的订单和销售展望仍显⽰，半导体设备需求具有较强韧
性。但对于芯⽚制造商和 AI 基础设施企业⽽⾔，市场开始要求更充分的证据，证明持续增加的资

### Page 4

về đóng cửa 1.737 điểm — giảm khoảng 2% so với tuần trước và đang kiểm định lại vùng 
đáy cũ của tháng 8, quanh 1.720. Áp lực bán duy trì khi chỉ số giảm nhiều phiên liên tiếp, 
cho thấy hiệu ứng "mua kỳ vọng, bán thực tế" sau nâng hạng vẫn chưa kết thúc, trong bối 
cảnh dòng tiền thận trọng và thanh khoản ở mức thấp. Khối ngoại tiếp tục là lực cản 
chính khi bán ròng mạnh cả 5 phiên trong tuần, riêng phiên 2/10 bán ròng tới 3.361 tỷ 
đồng, tập trung ở PNJ và HDB. Áp lực bên ngoài vẫn hiện diện khi Fed duy trì lập trường 
diều hâu và giá dầu ở mức cao; trong một, hai tuần tới, mùa báo cáo KQKD sẽ bắt đầu có 
tác động, kỳ vọng theo chiều hướng tích cực, qua đó giúp nâng đỡ thị trường chung.

Ngưỡng kỹ thuật

VNINDEX hình thành cây nến giảm điểm tiêu cực trên khung tuần, xuyên thủng vùng hỗ trợ 
gần quanh 1750, cho thấy áp lực bán đã có phần quyết liệt hơn sau 2 tuần giằng co trước 
đó. Trên khung ngày, sau các phiên lình xình vào đầu tuần, chỉ số cũng dần chuyển sang 
diễn biến giảm điểm rõ nét hơn về cuối tuần, đồng thời cắt sâu xuống dưới SMA50, thể 
hiện áp lực phân phối đang có phần lấn át mặc dù chưa xảy ra hiện tượng bán tháo khi 
khối lượng khớp lệnh được giữ ở mức thấp. Diễn biến tính từ vùng đỉnh 1870 vào đầu tháng 
9 đang không mấy khả quan, tuy nhiên xu hướng tăng ngắn hạn, tính từ đáy 1640 vào cuối 
tháng 7, hiện vẫn đang được bảo lưu và VNINDEX được kỳ vọng sẽ có nhịp hồi phục trở lại 
khi về đến vùng hỗ trợ kế tiếp tại quanh 1710. 
Kháng cự xa: 1820 (+-15) 
Kháng cự gần: 1775 (+-10) 
Hỗ trợ gần: 1710 (+-10) 
Hỗ trợ xa: 1670 (+-15)

Danh mục đầu tư mẫu

Phương pháp tiếp cận: 
- Danh mục tập hợp các cổ phiếu tiềm năng nhất được KBSV 
lựa chọn và tái cơ cấu theo tháng. 
- Ngưỡng cắt lỗ cần được cân nhắc cho mỗi mã là -15%. 
- Danh mục giả định luôn duy trì tỷ trọng 100% là cổ phiếu với 
trọng số được phân bổ đều giữa các mã.

Lưu ý:  
- NĐT được khuyến nghị mua tại mức giá bằng hoặc thấp hơn 
giá khuyến nghị mua. 
- Lợi nhuận kỳ vọng được tính theo giá khuyến nghị mua. 
 So sánh hiệu suất với VN30 Index

Document 233: CTCP Nhựa Bình Minh - Báo cáo cập nhật 2Q2026 (2026-09-23)

Source PDF: all report pdf/CTCP Nhựa Bình Minh - Báo cáo cập nhật 2Q2026 (2026-09-23).pdf

### Page 1

Doanh thu 2Q2026 tăng trưởng chậm 
nhưng biên lợi nhuận gộp vẫn được 
duy trì do tăng giá bán

Sản lượng tiêu thụ 6M2026 chững lại 
do tâm lý nhập hàng thận trọng và 
hoạt động xây dựng phục hồi chậm

Biên lợi nhuận gộp được dự báo sẽ 
thu hẹp cuối năm, phản ánh chi phí 
nguyên liệu cao hơn trong 1Q2026

Khuyến nghị TRUNG LẬP, giá mục 
tiêu 166,100 VND/cổ phiếu 
 Doanh thu/LNST 2Q2026 đạt lần lượt 1,320/367 tỷ VND (+1%/+11% YoY). Lũy kế 
6M2026, doanh thu đạt 2,777 tỷ VND (+3% YoY) trong khi LNST ước đạt 671 tỷ VND 
(+9% YoY). Biên lãi gộp 6M2026 cải thiện đạt 47.0% (+240 bps YoY), chủ yếu do việc 
tăng giá bán giúp doanh nghiệp bù đắp được mức tăng của chi phí đầu vào.

Lũy kế 6M2026, sản lượng tiêu thụ của BMP ước đạt 43,920 tấn, giảm 5% svck. Việc 
BMP thu hẹp các chương trình chiết khấu, với mức chiết khấu bình quân khoảng 
5.2% trong 2Q2026, làm giảm động lực nhập hàng của các đại lý và khiến sản lượng 
tiêu thụ chậm lại. Dù vậy, thị phần toàn quốc của BMP ước đạt 28% (+1 đpt svck) 
nhờ các doanh nghiệp khác trong ngành gặp khó khăn khi giá hạt nhựa tăng mạnh.

Biên lợi nhuận gộp của BMP dự kiến thu hẹp trong 2H2026 trước áp lực chi phí 
nguyên liệu đầu vào tăng. Tuy nhiên, chúng tôi kỳ vọng mức độ ảnh hưởng sẽ thấp 
hơn so với các doanh nghiệp trong ngành do (1) các lô nguyên liệu giá cao từ 
1Q2026 đã được đưa vào sản xuất và tiêu thụ một phần trong 1H2026; (2) BMP chủ 
động nâng gấp đôi hàng tồn kho để ứng phó với biến động bất lợi của giá nguyên 
liệu; (3) hơn 90% nhu cầu nguyên liệu uPVC được cung ứng từ các đối tác trong 
nước như hệ sinh thái SCG tại Việt Nam, AGC Chemicals và LSP; và (4) công ty duy 
trì giá bán bình quân ở mức cao.

Dựa trên kết quả kinh doanh và định giá, chúng tôi khuyến nghị TRUNG LẬP với cổ 
phiếu BMP, giá mục tiêu là 166,100 VND/cổ phiếu (tương ứng với mức tăng 14.3% 
so với giá đóng cửa ngày 23/09/2026).

Tăng trưởng doanh thu và lợi nhuận thuần 5 năm gần nhất Hoạt động kinh doanh

Nhựa Bình Minh (BMP) là doanh nghiệp hàng đầu 
trong lĩnh vực sản xuất ống nhựa với thị phần 
dẫn đầu tại thị trường nội địa (27% vào cuối 
2023), thị trường chính tập trung tại khu vực 
miền Nam. BMP có 4 nhà máy với tổng công suất 
đạt 150,000 tấn/năm. 
Nguồn: BMP, KBSV

Điểm nhấn đầu tư  Chú thích 
Kỳ vọng đà phục hồi của xây dựng dân dụng và bất động sản miền Nam sẽ thúc đẩy sản lượng tiêu 
thụ tăng trưởng trong 2026–2027. 
 Chi tiết ở phần dưới của báo cáo 
Giá PVC được kỳ vọng sẽ hạ nhiệt và khả năng giữ giá bán đầu ra ở mức cao giúp BMP giữ biên lợi 
nhuận gộp ở nền cao. 
 Chi tiết ở phần dưới của báo cáo

### Page 4

4

Nhu cầu nhập hàng thận trọng và 
cắt giảm các đợt chiết khấu trong 
kỳ của BMP làm giảm sản lượng tiêu 
thụ trong 2Q2026

Sự hồi phục của nhu cầu xây dựng 
dân dụng và nguồn cung các dự án 
bất động sản tại miền Nam sẽ thúc 
đẩy tăng trưởng sản lượng 2H2026 
 Sản lượng tiêu thụ trong 2Q2026 ước đạt 20,100 tấn, giảm 11% svck, nâng tổng 
sản lượng tiêu thụ 6M2026 ước đạt 43,920 tấn, giảm 5% svck do các yếu tố bất 
lợi từ bên ngoài như (1) giá hạt nhựa tăng cao khiến thị trường dè chừng trong 
việc nhập hàng vì lo ngại nguồn cung bất ổn (2) BMP đã thu hẹp các chương 
trình chiết khấu so với 2025 ngay trước thời điểm xung đột bùng nổ, giúp tỷ lệ 
chi phí bán hàng/doanh thu về mức 11.3%, giảm 800 bps so với 1Q2026 ở mức 
19.3%, nhưng đồng thời cũng khiến sản lượng tiêu thụ của BMP suy giảm do 
chính sách bán hàng thắt chặt hơn với các đối thủ có thị phần thấp hơn.

Chúng tôi kỳ vọng sản lượng tiêu thụ của BMP dần hồi phục từ 3Q2026 khi nhu 
cầu nhập hàng của các đại lý cải thiện sau giai đoạn thận trọng trong nửa đầu 
năm. Về cuối năm, hoạt động xây dựng dự kiến tăng tốc khi điều kiện thi công 
thuận lợi hơn sau mùa mưa. Bên cạnh đó, việc triển khai các dự án nhà ở tại 
TP.HCM và các tỉnh lân cận được kỳ vọng hỗ trợ nhu cầu ống nhựa trong giai 
đoạn tới. Theo đó, chúng tôi dự phóng sản lượng tiêu thụ của BMP lần lượt đạt 
96,695/100,125/96,750 tấn trong 2026/2027/2028.

Biểu đồ 3. Tương quan giữa tỷ lệ chi phí bán hàng/doanh 
thu và lợi nhuận thuần/sản lượng tiêu thụ  
 Biểu đồ 4. Dự phóng nguồn cung bất động sản tại Hà Nội và Hồ 
Chí Minh

Nguồn: BMP, KBSV  Nguồn: CBRE, KBSV

   BMP duy trì vòng quay dẫn đầu trong 
ngành nhờ lợi thế nguồn nguyên liệu và thị 
phần đứng đầu tại miền Nam. Tuy nhiên, 
xu hướng suy giảm các quý gần đây về 
mức 5.3 lần, giảm 0.21 điểm QoQ do nhu 
cầu tiêu thụ toàn ngành suy giảm.

### Page 5

5

Lợi thế từ hệ sinh thái công ty mẹ 
SCG và đối tác Nhật Bản giúp BMP 
có nhiều dư địa để nâng cao hiệu 
quả hoạt động

Theo chia sẻ từ ban lãnh đạo, BMP có lợi thế vì nhập nguyên liệu từ công ty mẹ 
là hệ sinh thái SCG tại Việt Nam và đối tác Nhật Bản là AGC Chemicals Vietnam, 
giúp giảm được 5% thuế nhập khẩu và chi phí vận chuyển. Điều này giúp BMP 
hưởng lợi kép khi có thể tăng được giá bán theo thị trường nhưng nguyên vật 
liệu rẻ hơn so với các doanh nghiệp trong ngành. KBSV nhận thấy định hướng 
dài hạn của BMP là cân bằng lợi ích cổ đông và khách hàng, vì vậy việc giảm giá 
bán để giành thị phần không phải lựa chọn ưu tiên của doanh nghiệp mà là 
nâng cao tỷ suất lợi nhuận/sản phẩm thành phẩm.

KBSV dự báo biên lợi nhuận gộp thu hẹp trong 2H2026 khi chi phí nguyên liệu 
giá cao từ 1H2026 được phản ánh vào giá vốn, và hồi phục trong 2027–2028 
nhờ đặc tính giữ giá bán ít thay đổi của nhóm nhựa. Chúng tôi kỳ vọng giá PVC 
resin chịu áp lực giảm từ (1) nhu cầu xây dựng tại Trung Quốc còn yếu khi thị 
trường bất động sản chưa hồi phục rõ ràng; và (2) các nhà sản xuất giải phóng 
hàng tồn kho sau mùa mưa, làm tăng sản lượng hạt nhựa PVC resin. Theo đó, 
KBSV dự phóng biên lợi nhuận gộp của BMP lên 46.4%/46.7%/46.5% trong 
2026/2027/2028.

gộp của BMP

EUROZONE: IMPROVING BUSINESS SENTIMENT, DRIVEN BY TECH, IS SPREADING TO OTHER SECTORS
Stéphane Colliac & Tarik Rharrab
*LATEST OBSERVATION: 5 OCTOBER 2026  **ESTIMATE
The September PMI surveys confirm that growth in the Eurozone is getting stronger, boosted in particular by new export orders, which are
still rising. Sectors that were already performing well – technology and capital goods (including for defence) – are continuing to grow, while

those that had been struggling until now – aeronautics, consumer goods and financial services – are seeing their production indices recover.
This strong performance is particularly noteworthy, given that it has not yet been undermined by higher inflation.
The heatmap shows the PMI index readings: a reading above 50 indicates expansion, while a reading below 50 signals contraction compared to the previous
month. For each indicator, sectors are ranked in descending order based on their September 2026 reading.

Shades of red represent values above 50, while shades of blue represent values below 50: the intensity of the colour increases the further away they are
from this threshold.
Production growth spreads to more sectors
The PMI surveys on business sentiment confirm that business activity in the Eurozone is expanding at a faster pace and spreading to more sectors.
Household-consumption-related sectors (goods and services), which were adversely affected in the spring by the outbreak of the conflict in the Middle

East, do not appear to be negatively impacted by the rising inflation observed since August. The aeronautics sector, where production has mainly been
limited by supply constraints, is also showing signs of improvement.
SOURCE: S&P GLOBAL, BNP PARIBAS

These trends, although still moderate, come alongside a drive to upgrade equipment, fuelled by investment in digital technology, electrification
(which is supporting the automotive sector) and defence. The momentum in technology goods and services further strengthened in September.
The production index for computer and electronics hit a five-year high, while the software services production index stayed close to its July peak.

Financial services are also enjoying a significant improvement, reflecting the healthy lending momentum despite interest rates starting to rise
(see our analysis).
Growth driven by exports
Exports drove growth in the first half of 2026, both across the Eurozone as a whole (see our analysis) and among its key members (notably
Germany). This momentum is also increasing, as indicated by the ‘new export orders’ component of the various PMI indices. As was the case with

production, there has been a particularly noteworthy increase for capital goods (technology and machinery) and related services, particularly digital
services. This trend is playing out more broadly, as, in September, new export orders rose across all sub-sectors, with the exception of intermediate
goods and consumer goods, where contraction recently eased. There has been a significant improvement in the transport sector, particularly in
the aeronautics industry, which has major markets in Asia. Recent data on French exports show that aeronautics exports to this region are rising

once again.
The convergence between rising production and increasing export-order backlogs shows that Eurozone growth is based on an increasingly solid
foundation. Inflationary pressures are not holding it back for the time being.

Document 318: Nuclear energy_ One industry, many reactors Our latest primer explores the reactor technologies and hurdles shaping nuclear’s next chapter. 2 min read

Source PDF: all report pdf/Nuclear energy_ One industry, many reactors Our latest primer explores the reactor technologies and hurdles shaping nuclear’s next chapter. 2 min read.pdf
Report source: Bank of America
Date: 2026-09-01
Pages: 8
Status: ok

### Page 1

13022014     1

Sustainability 
Nuclear energy: One industry, many reactors 
29 September 2026

Key takeaways 
• As power demand grows, nuclear has returned to the forefront of the energy conversation. Yet the industry is far from one-size-
fits-all, encompassing a range of reactor technologies designed to serve different markets, applications and deployment models. 
• Nuclear power is evolving beyond traditional large-scale reactors. While proven designs continue to anchor utility-scale 
generation, small modular reactors (SMRs) and microreactors are being developed to serve a broader range of applications 
through more flexible, modular deployment models, though most remain in the demonstration phase today. 
• The future of nuclear deployment will depend on more than reactor design alone. Supply chains, skilled labor, fuel availability and 
regulatory approvals remain critical challenges, though recent policy initiatives are intended to support licensing, fuel production 
and broader industry growth.

Nuclear energy has re-emerged at the center of the power conversation, driven by rising electricity demand and tightening 
supply-demand dynamics across the grid. Yet nuclear is not a single technology. It encompasses a wide range of reactor designs 
and sizes, each with distinct economics, risks and deployment pathways. Building off our previous nuclear publication (which can 
be found here: Nuclear energy: Who, what, where, when, why?), this primer unpacks that complexity, grounding the broader 
nuclear narrative in the practical realities of development, from technology and construction to fuel, licensing and execution.  
Reactor sizes 
Nuclear power is best understood as a collection of reactor classes differentiated by scale, deployment model and end use 
(Exhibit 1). Rather than competing directly with one another, these technologies are designed to serve different segments of the 
power market and, in many cases, can scale alongside one another. 
Exhibit 1: Different reactor types have different advantages and risks 
Comparison of reactor technologies

Source: BofA Global Research  
BANK OF AMERICA INSTITUTE 
I NSTI TUTE
Accessible version

### Page 2

2 29 September 2026 
  I NSTI TUTE

Key Distinctions  
• Large reactors (≥1 gigawatt (GW)): Designed for large-scale electricity generation, these reactors provide consistent, high-
capacity power and serve as core assets within the grid. 
• Small modular reactors (SMRs)(~50–300 megawatts (MW)): Mid-scale systems designed for modular deployment across 
utilities, industrial users and large commercial customers, offering greater flexibility in siting and capacity expansion.  
• Microreactors (≤20 MW): Small, highly distributed systems intended for niche or off-grid applications where reliability, 
autonomy or energy security are primary considerations. 
Technology 
Nuclear power generation is based on a common principle: heat produced from nuclear fission is used to generate steam, which 
drives a turbine to create electricity (Exhibit 2).  
Exhibit 2: In a nuclear reactor, heat is formed by fission 
Process for a nuclear fission reaction

Source: BofA Global Research 
BANK OF AMERICA INSTITUTE 
Nuclear reactor designs can be grouped based on coolant type, neutron spectrum and fuel configuration. Most reactors currently 
operating in the US are light-water reactors (LWRs), though a wide range of advanced designs are being developed (Exhibit 3).  
Exhibit 3: Gen III & Gen IV+ technologies have different characteristics 
Reactor technologies 
Reactor Type Coolant Moderator Neutron Spectrum Operating Temperature Pressure 
Pressurized Water Reactor (PWR) Water Light water Thermal ~300–330°C High (~150 bar) 
Boiling Water Reactor (BWR) Water Light water Thermal ~280–290°C High (lower than PWR) 
Heavy Water Reactor (HWR) Heavy water Heavy water Thermal ~300°C High 
Molten Salt Reactor (MSR) Molten salt Graphite (typical) Thermal / fast (design-dependent) ~600–750°C Low 
High-Temperature Gas Reactor (HTGR) Helium gas Graphite Thermal ~700–800°C Low 
Sodium Fast Reactor (SFR) Liquid sodium None Fast ~450–550°C Low 
Gas-Cooled Fast Reactor (GFR) Helium gas None Fast ~700–850°C Low 
Source: BofA Global Research, Energy Information Administration (EIA), Idaho National Laboratory (INL), Nuclear Regulatory Commission (NRC) 
BANK OF AMERICA INSTITUTE 
AP1000 
The AP1000 is a Generation III+ pressurized water reactor with a capacity of approximately 1.1 GW per unit (Exhibit 4). Its design 
is centered around passive safety systems that rely on gravity, natural circulation and compressed gas, rather than large numbers 
of active pumps and diesel-backed safety systems. By reducing the number of safety-critical moving parts, this approach lowers 
system complexity and supports a more standardized large-reactor platform.

### Page 3

29 September 2026    3

I NSTI TUTE

Exhibit 4: AP1000s typically need about 1,000 acres of land 
Diagram of an AP1000 nuclear plant

Source: BofA Global Research, Nuclear Regulatory Commission (NRC) 
BANK OF AMERICA INSTITUTE 
The AP1000’s design emphasizes open-top construction, with large structural and equipment modules manufactured offsite and 
then assembled at the project site. This approach is intended to reduce onsite labor needs, improve quality control through 
factory fabrication and support a more repeatable, standardized construction process. 
The AP1000 is no longer a conceptual technology. It is a licensed, utility-scale nuclear design already operating in the field. As a 
result, the key remaining considerations center on construction costs, project execution and the economics of deploying 
additional units, according to BofA Global Research. 
SMRs 
Small modular reactors (SMRs) are nuclear power plants generally 300 MWe or less per unit and are designed around a factory-
based, modular construction model (Exhibit 5). Unlike traditional gigawatt-scale nuclear projects, SMRs represent a shift toward 
repeatable, standardized deployment (read our prior SMR publication: Q&A quick guide: Small modular reactors). Major 
components can be manufactured in a factory setting and then transported to project sites for assembly, reducing the amount of 
onsite construction required. Their standardized designs also create opportunities for learning-by-doing, potentially lowering 
costs over time. In addition, capacity can be added incrementally through discrete modules, allowing capital to be deployed in 
stages rather than committed upfront.

### Page 4

4 29 September 2026 
  I NSTI TUTE

Exhibit 5: SMRs typically require 50-200 acres of land, depending on design and location 
Diagram of an SMR

Source: BofA Global Research, Nuclear Regulatory Commission (NRC) 
BANK OF AMERICA INSTITUTE 
Market landscape  
There are dozens of SMR designs globally, with more than 100 projects at various stages of development, though only a limited 
number are currently operational or under construction. While developers are pursuing a range of reactor technologies, light-
water reactor (LWR) designs still dominate the US development landscape (Exhibit 6). According to BofA Global Research, the 
sector remains largely in a development and demonstration phase rather than broad commercial deployment. 
Exhibit 6: LWRs make up 50% of the SMR designs 
US SMR designs by technology type (%)

Source: Energy Information Administration (EIA), BofA Global Research 
BANK OF AMERICA INSTITUTE
